# Review log: Sidecar runtime processes have no platform supervisor

## Standing context

- **Pass after round 39 (2026-10-23).** The ledger entries added since the previous pass, `[spec.39.fix-G1.1]` through `[spec.39.review-single-source.1]`, were lifted; the entries through `[prune38.2026-10-23]` were already curated by the previous pass and are stated here unchanged except where round 39 corrects them. The Ledger was not edited.

New Traps **§4.7.1 insert mirrors the end events**, **Claim pointer pair**, **§17.4 heredoc schema**, and **Declined in round 39**; **Shell traps** now names the round-39 snapshot. New Open lines **Runtime exits intro citation** and **§18.33 podsecurity list**; **R16 drain write** gained round-39 ids.

### Settled

**Scope and owner decisions**

- **Re-baseline of 2026-10-22.** The 2026-10-01 draft was re-baselined against `f52fc9a49` and the plan's Phase 2 entry; `[rebaseline.2026-10-22]` lists each change and each stale plan statement, and every staged edit quotes current tree text.
- **Part 2 split.** Cross-tenant reuse, the tenant-pin reset, and `allowCrossTenantReuse` live in proposal 0096; 0087 keeps a one-line pointer (D-PART2).
- **Owner decisions of 2026-10-22.** The owner approved D-SCOPE (only `type: agent` sidecar runtimes are supervised; `type: mcp` stays unsupervised and without `command` until the proposal that defines its launch, the follow-up in gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2), D-COMMAND (`command` and `env` only on the Runtime CRD), and D-RESTARTGATE. Do not reopen any of the three.
- **Restart gate (D-RESTARTGATE).** Pool admission refuses `recycle.runtimeProcess: restart` until proposal 0084 lands: code-only in CODE-5 `validateRecyclePolicy`, `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, after the `restartable` refusal, with a comment citing the intra-pod MCP nonce rule and stating that 0084 removes it; a tier-1 test, skip-register entries above tier 1, checklist S17 and S20, and the 0084 impacts row. No staged spec text states the refusal.
- **`command` and `env` on the CRD only.** Following the `requireSoPeercred` precedent; the admin payload models neither; `command` is required on a `type: agent` runtime whose `deploymentModel` is `sidecar`, and the CEL rule reads an absent `deploymentModel` as sidecar.
- **Registry mirror (D-RESTARTABLE).** The RuntimeReconciler mirrors only the derived flag `restartable` (home: §5.1 **`command`**); `applyCRDFields` copies neither `command` nor `env`; the SPEC-1(c) merge-table rows for `command` and `env` stay, because that table defines the effective runtime.
- **`restartable` predicate.** Carries `command`, `executionMode: session`, and no `capabilities.preConnect: true`; home SPEC-1(b); it excludes embedded, `type: mcp`, preConnect, service-mode, and admin-only records in one predicate.
- **Derived runtimes.** Admin-API-only (the Runtime CRD has no `baseRuntime` and requires `image`); `runtimestore.Merge` takes `Restartable` from the base, and CODE-5's restart gate reads the resolved runtime.
- **Lifetime field.** `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep | restart` is required with no default on every reusing recycling pool, concurrent pools included, and rejected elsewhere; `restart` is refused on a runtime that is not `restartable` and, on a sequential pool, is exempt from `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` (`acknowledgeBestEffortScrub` still applies).
- **Reusing recycling pool.** Defined once in SPEC-5(b) (`recycle.enabled`, `maxSessionsPerPod` above 1, `scrubProfile` other than `vm-restart`); the acknowledgment rule, the reuse predicate, the YAML comment, and D-LIFETIME cite it.
- **Runtime-process lifetime terms (second redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-5(b) §5.2 **Runtime-process lifetime (`recycle.runtimeProcess`).** defines "a `keep` pod" and "a `restart` pod" as pods whose lifetime, fixed at creation from the pool's field (or `keep` on a pool without it) and carried by `lenny.dev/runtime-process`, is `keep` or `restart`; the pool field is named only at pod creation, pool admission, and the acknowledgment requirement.
- **Pod lifetime carrier (D-PODLIFETIME).** The WarmPoolController writes `lenny.dev/runtime-process` at creation; §17.2 item 5 makes it immutable (`DecideImmutableLabels` also refuses unset to value); the adapter learns its lifetime from `--runtime-process`; no proto field is added.
- **Absent label reads `keep`.** An acquisition reads an absent or unknown label as `keep`, the refusing side; this fail-closed read is not redundant with the controller default.
- **Every `restart` pod is supervised.** `restart` needs `restartable`, which needs `command`, which only a `type: agent` sidecar runtime carries; embedded and `type: mcp` pods are always `keep` pods.
- **Lifetime refusal sites.** The §5.2 acknowledgment paragraph is the one home of which acquisitions refuse a used `keep` pod (the rebind and the Postgres fallback included); the hold and drain sites (§4.6.1, §4.6.3, §5.2 pinned idle, §6.2, §29.2 step 7) cite the per-pod refusal (SPEC-5(h)).
- **Rebind label read.** `Claimer.rebindReserved` reads no Pod today; CODE-5 adds the label read through a helper shared with `AdmitTenantPin`, and the rebind stamps no drain request (the next idle scan's `DrainRefusedPod` does).
- **Fallback claim reads the label.** The Postgres-backed fallback calls `AdmitTenantPin`, a direct Pod `Get` inside the row lock, so `agent_pod_state` needs no lifetime column.
- **Immutable-label list home.** §17.2 item 5; SPEC-5 deletes the §4.6.1 copy and reduces the §16.5 `LabelImmutabilityWebhookUnavailable` row to a citation; the §13.2 **Label immutability note** stays unedited because it is non-exhaustive.
- **Pool-keyed lifetime sites.** SPEC-5(h) rewrites the §5.2 YAML comment and acknowledgment bullet, §5.2 **Client visibility of weak-isolation reuse.**, two §6.1 paragraphs, the §7.1 `residualStateWarning` row, and the §13.1 `lenny-cred-readers` paragraph; requirement sites cite the acknowledgment and residual-state sites say "on a `keep` pod".
- **Tenant pin.** SPEC-5(h) extends pin persistence to every pool and drops the kept-process rationale; no control changes.

**Supervisor build, trust, and CH-SUPERVISE**

- **Owner decisions of 2026-10-22 on the supervisor's security review (fourth redesign).** Sender identification is retired everywhere (no `SA_SIGINFO`, no `si_pid` read, no sender property); fault signals have no handler; the implementation hazards are CODE-2 requirements with TEST-1 cases; the `setsid`/`setpgid` escape from the cleanup deadline is an accepted bound stated once in SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** Do not reopen any of these.
- **Supervisor binary.** Freestanding static C with no libc in its own digest-pinned image, staged by init container `lenny-supervisor-stage` at a dedicated UID into a volume mounted at `/lenny-supervisor`, outside `/run/lenny`; D-BINARY excludes Go for its fault-signal handlers and threads.
- **Implementation hazards (CODE-2).** SIGCHLD flag cleared before the reap loop; close-on-exec on every descriptor; `EINTR` retry on every syscall; a flag-only handler for every signal 1 to `_NSIG - 1` other than SIGKILL, SIGSTOP, and the fault signals; no `alarm` or `setitimer`; the kill-reach self-test passes only when the grandchild's `/proc` entry exists before `kill(-1)` and is gone after.
- **SPEC-6 mount rule.** No container other than `lenny-supervisor-stage` mounts the supervisor volume read-write; the volume name stays an implementation constant; `lenny-pod-security` already intercepts ephemeral containers.
- **Environment home.** SPEC-2 **Environment.**: image ENTRYPOINT, CMD, and ENV unused; fixed `PATH`; CRD `env` with the `LENNY_` prefix reserved; platform variables; set after `clearenv()`; the supervisor receives them in argv (`run --env K=V ... -- ARGV`).
- **Property self-test carrier (second redesign of 2026-10-22).** The supervisor always dials and sends `ready` first with `properties`; the adapter logs each failure and, on a `restart` pod, exits non-zero before READY when non-dumpable inspection or kill reach failed; PID-namespace init immunity is reported only (D-PROPERTIES); home SPEC-4 item 8 **Property self-test.**
- **Preflight (SPEC-13).** `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor` runs the `selftest` mode for a named RuntimeClass, with create, get, and delete on pods and get on `pods/log`; CODE-6 reads the result lines from the pod log.
- **CH-SUPERVISE authority.** `Both`, because `ready` and `exited` are unsolicited.
- **CH-SUPERVISE exclusivity.** The card's **Exclusivity.** bullet is the home: agent-pod `restartPolicy: Never` first, the adapter closes its listener after the first pre-READY accept, and the supervisor exits when the connection closes; §28.6 and §28.8 point at it; SPEC-7(h) removes the agent-pod `CrashLoopBackOff` claims at §4.7.11 item 1 step 4 and §10.3.
- **CH-SUPERVISE dial.** The supervisor repeats only a refused dial (`ECONNREFUSED`) up to `--dial-timeout` (default at least the adapter's `AwaitSupervisor` wait); an accepted connection that fails the adapter-UID check exits without a retry.
- **CH-SUPERVISE endpoint.** No manifest-nonce handshake; the reason is §4.7.11 item 8 **Why each end trusts the other.**, and the false "before any manifest exists" clause is deleted.
- **CH-SUPERVISE requests.** One outstanding request; `ping` only when no other request is outstanding; every request bounded by the deadline of the operation that sends it; an unanswered request is a link failure.
- **CH-SUPERVISE unnamed members.** The **Messages.** bullet states that the specification does not name the members of `cleanup` and `exited`, the property names, or the error codes; CODE-2 lists them as IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE and the TEST-1 tier-3 contract test pins them.
- **§28.6 wording.** SPEC-7(e) changes "one per-pod constraint" to "a per-pod constraint" beside the new **One connection per pod.** paragraph.
- **Cleanup bound names (prune of 2026-10-22).** The `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` deadline (worker SIGKILLs the running command's process group) is defined in SPEC-2 **Cleanup.**; the `cleanup` request bound (exceeds that deadline; unanswered is a link failure) is defined in the card's **Timing.** bullet. Do not write "the deadline of the `cleanup` request" or "the request bound" for either.

**Homes in SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 and §5.2 (cited elsewhere, never restated)**

- **SDK-warm lifecycle home (redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-2 **Runtime supervisor.** **SDK-warm.**; **Launch.** is the one home of the warm-up READY gate. §4.7.10 lifetime, **End.**, SPEC-3(a), SPEC-8(d), SPEC-9(a), SPEC-9(c), SPEC-9(d), D-SDKWARM, and CODE-3 cite it.
- **SDK-warm decisions.** `DemoteSDK` sends `end` only, with no `sweep`, and keeps 0090's order and fail-closed rule; a warm-up launch happens only when the SDK-warm breaker was closed at pod creation (`--launch-mode`, CODE-3 **Adapter inputs**); a `ConfigureWorkspace` that finds no live process launches at step 7; the re-warm edge launches nothing; `PreConnect` runs once.
- **SDK-warm actor.** SPEC-9(a) §6.1: "The adapter starts the SDK process during warm-up ..., in the sidecar model through the runtime supervisor"; §5.1 `capabilities.preConnect` is passive with a §6.1 pointer; SPEC-9(e) qualifies the §6.3 boot-step wording.
- **Launch home.** SPEC-2 **Launch.**: launch points and the permit (no process launched since creation or the last attested end); a start while a process is live or a launch is in flight uses that process; READY waits for the warm-up process's connection, and a warm-up failure exits the adapter before READY; SPEC-3(b) step 7 cites **Launch.** by pointer.
- **End and rebind (D-STALE).** After an attested end the adapter closes and rebinds only `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`; 0087 does not render `--runtime-ops-socket` (R17 does); one `SocketRuntimeProcess` per launch (D-TRANSPORT), because `CloseListener` leaves it sticky-ended.
- **Generation.** The §28.6 "given exactly one session" MCP count is per generation, and the adapter is its trusted source.
- **Keep-pod lifetime.** Stated only in SPEC-2(a), with its `DemoteSDK` exception; the trade-off cell, the `Shutdown` row (", which stays alive for the pod's life" deleted), and SPEC-5(g) ("can run") point at it.
- **Restart-pod end.** A `restart` pod's process ends in each whole-pod scrub (SPEC-2(a) and SPEC-5(b) cite §5.2 **Lenny scrub procedure.**), or when the pod retires without a scrub.
- **Deleted lifetime sentence.** SPEC-2(a) deletes the §4.7.10 "runtime process that stops ... is not re-created" sentence; §10.1 **Hold state timeout:** points at **Liveness.** (SPEC-2(e)); §11.4 step 3 loses "does not end the runtime process".
- **ReportPodScrub row.** SPEC-2(d) replaces the row's predicate with a pointer to **Liveness.** and says "runtime" rather than "runtime process".
- **Runtime not live home.** SPEC-5(i) re-keys §5.2 **Runtime not live:** to "its runtime" and appends a citation sentence on that line; **Liveness.** says "reports the runtime as not able to serve".
- **Runtime sweep home (redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-2 **Sweep.**, whose volume list is the runtime container's writable mounts (`/sessions`, `/artifacts`, `/tmp`, `/dev/shm`, and `/workspace` other than `slots/`, which step 2 clears); SPEC-5(c) to (e), D-SWEEP, and CODE-2 cite it.
- **Cleanup home.** SPEC-2 **Cleanup.**: agent UID, a forked worker, the workspace base, the environment, and an aggregate `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` bound; the worker stops at the first failed command; the keep-pod residue is its last sentence (the runtime can stop or signal the worker, and stop, signal, read, or trace the commands).
- **Cleanup on supervised pods (D-CLEANUP).** `cleanupCommands` run through the supervisor's worker in the runtime container on every supervised pod; embedded and `type: mcp` pods run them in the adapter.
- **Signal model home (third redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-2(b) **Signals.** and its table; no action depends on a signal's sender; the kubelet's SIGTERM gets no action, so the runtime lives until the adapter's `end` or exit; a sent fault signal stays pending or is discarded and never reaches a child, and a synchronous fault ends the supervisor. Do not reintroduce a sender filter.
- **Child-process model home (fourth redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-2(b) **Children.** and its table: one reaper collects every child, and a status that does not reach its waiting step is a failure; the scrub table's `cleanupCommands` Error cell, the SIGCHLD row, the edge-case table, D-CHILDREN, D-CLEANUP, CODE-2, and TEST-1 cite it.
- **Supervised scrub sequence home (third redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-5(c) §5.2 **Supervised scrub sequence.** table; SPEC-5(c)'s order sentence states only step 0, `cleanupCommands`, steps 1 to 6, and points to the table.
- **Scrub table rules.** A step-0 error skips the `cleanupCommands` row (gate in `scrub.Run` on every pod); the second end and sweep run after a failed or timed-out cleanup; a sweep bound-hit is not live and replies `swept` with an error code; a link failure skips every later request row; the listener rebind has a row whose failure is not live; the next launch runs only when **Launch.** permits and nothing reported not live; the release row runs on every supervised pod; the table has no exit column.
- **Scrub steps 1 and 1b on supervised pods (2026-10-22).** Steps 1 and 1b run in the adapter container on every pod, because setup commands run there at the adapter UID; SPEC-5(d) edits step 3 only, and SPEC-5(e) says "Steps 0 to 6 execute in the adapter's process" and "Steps 1 and 1b run on every pod ...".
- **SPEC-5(e) keep-pod sentence.** Reads "the runtime process and its memory persist across the boundary, and in the sidecar model so do the processes it started".
- **Crash home.** SPEC-2 **Crash.**: a crash is an end whose **Runtime exits.** row has Crash yes; the sessions that fail are those the process holds, from the rule-8 record to deregistration; the SPEC-2(d) §4.7.1 critical-section insert names the `exited` report, the `ended` reply, and the classified `CH-MSGSOCK` end as one step that chooses the row and applies its outcome (`[spec.39.fix-G1.1]`; `ended` can be the first handled event, because **Children.** sends no `exited` for an exit reaped during an `end`, and CODE-3 already handles `ended` under `s.mu`).
- **Runtime end table (owner delegation of 2026-10-23, narrowed by `[prune38.2026-10-23]`).** SPEC-2(b) **Runtime exits.** states the outcome of a runtime process's end (an `exited` report, an `ended` reply, or a `CH-MSGSOCK` end the adapter did not cause by its own exit or rebind). It has one row per value of CODE-3's `disposition` enum: `endDemote`, `endScrub`, `shutdown`, `holdTimeout`, `deleting`, and `none`; `none` covers what were the `runtime-exit`, `protocol`, and `supervisor-death` paths with one outcome, and its cell lists those causes. Columns: Set before, Crash, sessions and reason, not-live state, adapter exits; there is no Retirement column, and **Liveness.** is the one statement of retirement. Precedence is the enum order, stated once; `launch` resets to `none`; a generation's first handled event selects the row by the value then held. The adapter sets the disposition before every act by which it ends a generation. The table does not claim to enumerate every way a runtime can end; add a row only for a new enum value.
- **Runtime end rows.** `shutdown`: no crash, no cause, status meaning in §28.5.3 **Exit Codes**. `holdTimeout`: no crash, sessions end `coordinator_lost`, not-live through the *no connection* cause, the adapter does not exit except as **Liveness.** states for a cause. `deleting`: ends after the adapter set `deleting` (such as at its `end` during the drain); held sessions fail `runtime_crash`, no cause. `none`: a `restart` pod whose process exits by itself between its last release and the boundary's `end` retires (accepted).
- **`disposition` enum (CODE-3).** In precedence order `endDemote`, `endScrub`, `shutdown`, `holdTimeout`, `deleting`, `none`; set under `s.mu` before each act, never moved later in that order, reset by `launch`; it replaces `shutdownWritten`, `holdClosed`, and `terminating`. A generation launched after termination reached the adapter takes the `none` row.
- **Own closes (`[prune38.2026-10-23]`).** A close the adapter makes as it exits (any cause: a **Liveness.** cause, the end of termination, a fatal error, a panic, or an OOM kill) or for the rebind after an attested end is not a runtime end and reaches no handler. The adapter's own exit is covered by §6.2 **Pod crash during an active session.** and **Liveness.**, cited from the **Runtime exits.** intro and not restated. The `holdTimeout` close is an act that ends a generation and selects its row.
- **Not end paths.** A heartbeat escalation (`Interrupt` is a no-op on the sidecar transport), a launched process that never connects, a refused handshake, and a `CH-SUPERVISE` link failure while the supervisor runs end no process or accepted connection.
- **`shutdown` writer.** The tree writes no `CH-MSGSOCK` `shutdown` frame (R16 owns it); CODE-3 tier-1 cases set `disposition` directly; the tier-10 §15.4.6 shutdown case is written and skipped naming R16; `runShutdownDeadlineCheck` is unaffected.
- **Not-live latch home (fourth redesign of 2026-10-22; extended by the fifth).** SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** and its table state every cause (*hang* on every pod; *runtime exit* is the not-live effect of the `none` row), that the state refuses every start and every `launch`, that nothing clears it, and that `ReportPodScrub` reports it; before READY a cause exits the adapter, and on a service-mode pod any cause does; after READY no other pod's adapter exits for a cause. **Launch.**, **SDK-warm.**, SPEC-4 item 8, the scrub table, the edge-case table, D-NOTLIVE, and CODE-3 cite it.
- **Causes on every pod.** A heartbeat escalation (D-HUNG; closes F-5.2.46), the hold-timeout close, a launched process that does not connect within its starting session's deadline, a failed listener rebind, and a launched process that exits before it holds its first session.
- **Not-live reclaim table home (fifth redesign of 2026-10-22).** The table under **Liveness.**: rows are the `Sandbox` phases `warming`, warm-fill `sdk_connecting`, `idle`, `reserved`, `claimed` with a `bound` claim (sequential and concurrent), `claimed` with a `recycling` claim, re-warm `sdk_connecting`, and termination; columns service-mode, session `keep`, `restart`, `preConnect`; each cell names causes and one channel. §6.2 owns only the edges, which SPEC-2(f) extends with the termination of the adapter or runtime container on the warm-fill and re-warm `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` edges and `idle ──→ draining`. Add no §6.2 paragraph for the adapter's exit.
- **Retirement channels.** **Exit** (the WarmPoolController observes a terminated `adapter` or `runtime` container on a pod not being deleted; in `warming`, `sdk_connecting`, and `idle` a runtime-container termination retires the pod through **Exit** ahead of a cell's **Start**); **Start** (the next start, a rebind included, is refused); **Claim**; **Report** (`ReportPodScrub`); **None** (being deleted).
- **Liveness Claim channel (round 39).** The **Claim** bullet cites the §4.6.3 SandboxClaim ownership row for "ends the occupancy without reuse" and points at §5.2 **Runtime not live:** for the concurrent-pool trigger; the three "as the release of ... does" clauses are gone (`[spec.39.fix-G2.1]`, `[spec.39.fix-design-G2.1]`).
- **Refused start release.** A start refused while `notLive` is set fails before the pod accepts it, so no replica publishes its binding and its release is without reuse under the §4.6.3 row's binding clause; §4.6.3 is the only statement of the without-reuse predicate.
- **Start refusal atomicity (round 34, USEFUL in round 39).** The **Liveness.** start refusal need not be atomic with the §4.7.1 rule-8 record: a start recorded after an `exited` was handled still fails through *no connection*, the closed-connection write, or the 10 s heartbeat escalation, so no session runs on a dead process.
- **Warm-fill causes precede READY.** Warm-fill `sdk_connecting` ends when readiness flips (`DecideSDKWarm`), and READY waits for the warm-up connection, so every warm-fill `sdk_connecting` cause is before READY and the preConnect cell "Any cause → **Exit**" holds.
- **Latched pod with no session (owner delegation of 2026-10-22, revert).** A cause after READY on a session-mode pod holding no session (`idle`, `reserved`, re-warm `sdk_connecting`) does not exit the adapter; the pod retires through **Start**, or first through the optional uptime limit. Do not reintroduce an exit keyed on the registry, session history, or the scrub.
- **§6.2 fenced edits (SPEC-2(f)).** The warm-fill and re-warm `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` edges and `idle ──→ draining` gain "the adapter or runtime container terminates — see §4.7.10 Runtime supervisor"; SPEC-2(f) cites summary D-PREREQS for the precursor and narrates nothing.
- **Precursor WarmPoolController fix (owner decision of 2026-10-22).** `fix/warmpool-reclaim-terminated-adapter` (`c2acb6a41`, `14157668c`, `c29693b62`) lands before S1 and is a precondition: `observePod` returns `PodTerminating` for a deleted pod first, then `PodFailed` for a terminated `adapter` or `runtime` container; `Decide` drains `idle`; `DecideSDKWarm` fails `sdk_connecting` on both legs; `HoldFailedReclaim` holds `failed ──→ draining` while a claim exists; `warming` holds while terminating; `claimed` and `reserved` get no action. Only the `PodAbsent` divergence stays in the defects section. 0087 stages no change to `observePod`, `Decide`, or `DecideSDKWarm`.

**Shutdown, exit codes, and stderr**

- **Shutdown deadline home.** The §28.5.3 `CH-MSGSOCK` card **Timing.** bullet (the supervisor sends SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 10 s later); **Inbound: `shutdown`** is a pointer.
- **Exit Codes.** Rows 1 and 137 lose their outcome clauses; the paragraph stays in force on every pod and is the home of what a status after `shutdown` means; SPEC-8(b) appends that the supervisor reports the status on `CH-SUPERVISE` and that **Runtime exits.** states each end's outcome.
- **RUNTIME_CRASH.** SPEC-8(c) at the three §28 sites: synthesized from the exit status the supervisor reports, for an end whose **Runtime exits.** row is a crash; no embedded clause, because the embedded runtime is the adapter.
- **stderr.** No component captures runtime stderr after SPEC-8(d) (§15.4.1) and SPEC-8(e) (§4.4.1 **Stores:**); the §15.4 SIGKILL sites use the passive voice.

**§26, §29, and build sequence**

- **Reference runtimes need `command`.** The chart renders each `referenceRuntimes.catalog` entry as a `type: agent` Runtime CRD on the sidecar default; SPEC-1(e) adds the §26.1 pattern `["/usr/local/bin/lenny-<name>-adapter"]` and the §26.3 claude-code YAML line; CODE-1 owns the chart catalog and template; only the `lenny up` seed is admin-path.
- **§26.3 Bootstrap.** Step 2 reads "The platform adapter boots and signals READY."; the launch pointer sits in step 3 after `runs any `setupCommands`;`; the **Setup-time image entrypoint:** line is deleted.
- **§29.2 trace.** A `runtime supervisor` participant and a new step 25; old 25 to 34 become 26 to 35; SPEC-12(d) alone rewrites `(step 25)`; the first-session steps read "On the first session of the runtime process of a pod-warm pod"; nothing outside §29.2 cites its steps above 24.
- **Phase placement.** SPEC-14(a) puts the supervisor and its stage container in Phase 2, because the tier-7b startup-latency baseline runs pods.

**Surfaces, checklist, and impacts**

- **Proto.** No message, field, or RPC changes; CODE-3 rewrites the `Shutdown`, `ReportPodScrubRequest` `runtime_live`, and `RunSetupRequest` doc comments, regenerates `pkg/proto/adapter/v1`, and rewrites the `adapterclient` `Shutdown` comment; D-SURFACE matches.
- **Checklist.** Spec block S1 to S13, one SPEC id per step; non-spec steps S14 to S21; S6b updates `claimOwnershipRowSentence`; the precursor lands before S1.
- **Staged non-spec items.** CODE-4 `createPod` renders `restart` only for a `restartable` runtime (tier-1 case); a tier-5 service-mode replacement case with a FIXTURE-1 service-mode pool; CODE-5 retargets the hold-predicate comments (`sandboxclaim_types.go` and its CRDs, the `claimHoldTTLSeconds` comment, `claimer.go`, `tenantpin.go`, `kept_runtime_rule_test.go`); CODE-4 adds `lenny.dev/runtime-process` to `DecideImmutableLabels` with tier-1 and tier-9 cases.
- **0079 impact.** CODE-5 splits 0079's `KeepsRuntimeAcrossSessions` into `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` and `ReusesPods`; 0079 is Implemented (2026-10-03).
- **0096 impact.** Key the pin reset on the boundary's second `end` and `sweep`; a not-live pod stays `idle` and claimable until **Start**, so its phase alone does not attest it clean.
- **Prerequisites met.** 0090 to 0094 and the credential fail-open fix (`d05c25630`) landed; 0090 closed F-4.7.25; the egress-capture container left the agent UID with 0088.
- **Plan staleness.** Plan §10.2 and §10.3 are stale on F-4.7.25, the credential-fix merge, F-5.2.46's owner, the "can be supervised" flag (now `restartable`), "four properties", admin-only runtimes, supervision scope, D-STALE's subject, and a pod-level `runAsUser`; `[rebaseline.2026-10-22]` lists each.

**Tree facts**

- **Agent pod shape.** `restartPolicy: Never` (code only, `base_pod.go`); `adapter` then `runtime` as regular containers; no shared PID namespace; read-only root filesystems; the preStop hook only on the adapter; no probes on the runtime container.
- **Setup commands.** Run in the adapter process at the adapter UID (`Server.RunSetup` to `workspace.RunSetup`); the cancel kills only the process group; the adapter mounts the credential volume read-write.
- **Scrub bound.** The whole-pod scrub runs on `context.Background()`; the gateway missing-report timeout (`cleanupTimeoutSeconds` plus `MissingReportGracePeriod`, 15 s, operator-tunable) bounds it; CODE-3 requires a deadline on the scrub's supervisor requests.
- **MCP re-arm.** The code re-arms the intra-pod MCP surface on every sole-session start (`claimPodMCPStartLocked`), while the spec says the servers start once per pod; the mismatch belongs to 0084.
- **Recycle ordering.** The gateway patches the claim to `recycling` before the last-session `Shutdown` on every recycling pool; a concurrent pod's last release sends two `Shutdown`s.
- **Hold timeout.** `onHoldTimeout` closes the runtime connection, then pass 1 deregisters, then pass 2 terminates; the adapter does not exit.
- **Runtime environment.** The SDKs read `LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET`, which CODE-4 moves into the supervisor's platform `--env` pairs; the spec names no runtime variable.
- **SDK signal handling.** The Python and TypeScript runtime SDKs install no SIGTERM handler; Go runtimes die on SIGTERM.
- **`cleanupCommands` argv.** Run today in argv mode (whitespace split, no shell); the `cleanup` message carries argument lists.
- **Breaker reads.** The WarmPoolController reads `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` on every reconcile while the warm-up launch is fixed at creation; the drift converges.
- **WarmPoolController reads.** It reads pool values from the PSC-owned `SandboxTemplate.spec` (a `recycle` block exists), already reads the Runtime CRD under an existing grant, and label writes on agent pods are per-key merge patches.
- **Orphan reconciliation scope.** §10.1 **Orphan session reconciliation:** keys only on an `agent_pod_state` of `Terminated`, so it does not reclaim a session on a hold-timed-out pod whose adapter keeps running; pre-existing, not 0087's (`[spec.39.review-reliability.1]`).
- **Runtime-container writable mounts.** Exactly workspace, tmp, sessions, artifacts, and dshm (credential and shared volumes are read-only), matching the SPEC-2 **Sweep.** list.

**Round-39 checks (staging at `feef33ad4`)**

- **Find strings and rows.** Every SPEC-1 to SPEC-14 find string resolves once or at its stated count (two for "slot-identifier reclaim hold.", three for RUNTIME_CRASH); inserted rows match column counts (§28.3 9, §28.8 5, §24.2 4, §29.1 3); no retired row label remains in spec-changes.md.
- **Matrix bijection.** Tier-0 `matrix_completeness_test.go` needs the §28.3 register and §28.8 matrix in bijection; SPEC-7(b) and (f) add both `CH-SUPERVISE` rows in one step.
- **Banned-phrase scan.** No staged spec text matches `runtime_process_lifetime_doc_reconciliation_test.go`; the "The adapter spawns the runtime" hit is SPEC-11 preamble prose, never applied.
- **Anchors and labels.** Every linked anchor and cited bold label resolves; the §28.6 "given exactly one session" rule is in §28.6 itself; the §4.7.11 item 1 re-run is in its **Escalation requirement.** bullets; §4.7.11 items 2 and 3 are the ones SPEC-4 item 8 means; §13.1 **Container identity.** supports SPEC-6's UID sentence.
- **Client surface.** No REST, MCP, A2A, OpenAI-completions, proto, or JSONL contract changes; §15 has no pool-body schema needing `runtimeProcess`; §5.2 pool rejections name no error code; §4.4 "Key rules:" is non-exhaustive, so the `command` CEL rules need no entry.
- **Admin-path examples need no `command`.** The only spec-side Runtime CRD example is the §17.4 `runtime-crds.yaml` heredoc (SPEC-1(d)); the §17.4 step-3 manifest, §5.1 Minimal Configuration, and §17.6 bootstrap register through the admin path (D-COMMAND).
- **Registers outside the edit.** §28.7 derives from schemas/, and `CH-SUPERVISE` adds no schema; the §25 ImageResolver list omits the adapter image, so it is no image inventory; the naming table records only changed identifiers; the §28.3 `CH-SUPERVISE` row has nine columns and the card follows §28.5 field order.
- **Pool-keyed sites that stay true.** The §6.1 preConnect row ("the runtime process is kept"), the §5.1 setupCommands "kept across sessions" clause (conditional), and the §10.1 hold-timeout purpose clause.
- **Kubernetes writes.** The WarmPoolController alone writes `Sandbox.status`, the gateway alone writes `SandboxClaim` status and reads the Pod label under its existing `get`, the label is set once and immutable, and no session-start path waits on a reconcile.
- **Pruned plumbing.** Tier-0 and test plumbing, CODE-3 and CODE-4 registers and gates, the CODE-4 timing plumbing, and the pre-READY exit mechanism are IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE items constrained by invariants (`[prune-remedy.2026-10-23.7]`).

### Traps

- **Plan entry is stale.** The plan's Phase 2 entry (2026-10-12) and §10.3 carry stale statements (Settled **Plan staleness**); read the tree, not the entry, for current facts.
- **Distroless adapter image.** `scrub.DefaultOps` steps 1 and 1b exec `sh` and `ipcrm`, which the distroless adapter image lacks; CODE-3 implements both natively (`kill(-1)` and `IPC_RMID` by adapter UID) on every pod.
- **Restating a home.** Do not restate the crash predicate outside **Crash.**, an end path's outcome outside **Runtime exits.**, a scrub step's order or outcome outside **Supervised scrub sequence.**, a signal action outside **Signals.**, a child outcome outside **Children.**, or a cause or the refusal outside **Liveness.**. Each restatement so far (the registered-entry wording of rounds 32 and 33, the scrub table's exit column, SPEC-2(f)'s precursor narration, the Exit Codes status meaning) became the next finding. Cite **Runtime exits.** rows by label, never by ordinal.
- **Pointer-only cells.** Edge-case outcome cells and summary D-* entries are pointers: a cell names the bullet, table, row, or cause that states the outcome, and a D-* entry states the decision, one reason, and a pointer.
- **Admin-registered runtime.** An admin-only runtime has no pod path unless a Runtime CRD of the same name exists; the pod builder reads `command` from the CRD, and the reconciler mirrors only `restartable`. Do not stage `command` or `env` on the admin payload.
- **Tier-11 banned phrases.** `TestKeptRuntimeRetireConditionHasOneSpecHome` bans "cannot serve the next session" and "runtime_not_live" on every physical line of spec/04, 05, 06, and 29 except the §5.2 **Runtime not live:** line, which must keep `runtime_not_live` and "or omits that fact". Redesign 4 regressed **Liveness.** to "cannot serve the next session" though a FACT named the safe wording (MISTAKE, fixed round 28). A rewrap can create or hide a hit, so grep the staged blocks after every edit; the `retiredPerSessionProcessPhrases` hit in SPEC-11's preamble is not staged text.
- **Other tier-11 pins.** `runtime_session_end_paths_doc_test.go` pins lifecycle.md's "A runtime process that stops, or whose connection the hold timeout closed, ..." sentence, so the DOC-1 lifecycle.md rewrite updates that test in the same step; `claimOwnershipRowSentence` pins the §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row (S6b).
- **Shell traps.** `*spec-changes.md` in the proposal directory also matches `non-spec-changes.md`, so name the file in full; ugrep `-o` with long `.{0,N}` contexts fails on complexity, so use `tr '.' '\n' | grep`; the `spec-r39` snapshot differs from the live spec-changes.md from line 19 and is not the reviewed state, so see what changed since the round-38 pass with `git diff fdbe540cc HEAD -- <full spec-changes path>` (`[spec.39.review-plan-conformance.1]`, `[spec.39.review-docs-alignment.1]`).
- **Refuted single-source filings.** Grep `scratchpad/cp-log/0087-p1/history/spec.r{2,3,11,12}.history.md` before filing a (g) finding. Refuted repeatedly: the SPEC-5(g) double keep-pod refusal (rounds 2, 3, 11, 12); the **Pod termination.** and card **Degradation.** supervisor-exit sentence; the `restartable` predicate; the §28.8 row (a derived matrix by its intro); the **Liveness.** and card "no supervisor connects" pair; the **Runtime exits.** Adapter column against **Liveness.**; the Signals intro reason clause; the **Launch.** warm-up failure clause; the **Runtime exits.** intro's "`shutdown` only in DRAINING" clause.
- **Sidecar is not supervised.** "In the sidecar model" also covers an unsupervised `type: mcp` sidecar pod. A supervisor-dependent sentence says "a pod that runs the runtime supervisor" (code condition: non-empty `--supervise-socket`); "on a `restart` pod" needs no rescoping.
- **Keep-pod sentences cover embedded pods.** Every non-`restart` pod, embedded and `type: mcp` included, is a `keep` pod, so a keep-pod sentence true only of supervised pods is false on embedded pods. SPEC-5(e) dropped "in the sidecar model so do the processes it started" twice (MISTAKE `[spec.16.follow-up-fix.1]`, caught again in round 19); keep that qualifier in any rewrite.
- **Steps 1 and 1b on every pod.** Rounds before 16 exempted supervised pods from steps 1 and 1b and made `DefaultOps` a no-op under `--supervise-socket`, on the false premise that the adapter container runs no author process (MISTAKE). Setup commands run there at the adapter UID with the credential volume read-write, and a `setsid` descendant survives the process-group kill. Do not reintroduce the exemption, and do not add setup-command text to SPEC-5(g).
- **Embedded step 1b UID.** Round 1 retargeted embedded step 1b to the agent UID (MISTAKE); the embedded runtime runs at the adapter UID and §13.1 reserves the agent UID for the `runtime` container.
- **Deleted stopped-process sentence.** Rounds 1 and 2 each narrowed the §4.7.10 "runtime process that stops ... is not re-created" sentence, and each narrowing became the next finding; the embedded form had the stopped adapter report its own stop (MISTAKE). Do not reintroduce any form of it.
- **Unconditional lifetime claims.** Do not write "stays alive" or "lives as long as the pod" outside SPEC-2(a); every restatement omitted the `DemoteSDK` exception. The `Shutdown` row's second replacement (the process kept across the recycle boundary on a `keep` pod) is a boundary statement and stays without a DemoteSDK clause.
- **Restart end predicate.** "At each occupancy-zero boundary at which the pod is reused" is wrong on the end side, because the scrub ends the process before the gateway decides reuse; it stays true on the launch side only.
- **Lifetime wording.** Do not write "under `keep`", "under `restart`", "a `restart` pool", or a pool-keyed phrase in a lifetime-dependent sentence. After any lifetime fix, grep spec/ for "keeps its runtime process", "keep a runtime process", and "kept runtime process".
- **DemoteSDK and re-warm.** `DemoteSDK` sends `end` only, because a sweep at claim time deletes agent files during a claim. The re-warm edge launches nothing. Do not restore the `scrub_warning` launch block: under `onScrubFailure: warn` a pod serves the next session, so the block failed every warn-policy `restart` pod's next start.
- **MCP nonce belongs to 0084.** A relaunched process presents a rotated nonce that the spec's once-per-pod MCP servers refuse (§4.7.6 `mcpNonce`, §29.2 step 26a, §15.4.3); the code re-arms per sole start. It is a recorded non-goal gated by D-RESTARTGATE: do not re-file it or its `DemoteSDK`-relaunch variant on `keep` preConnect pods, and stage no nonce re-arm. FIXTURE-1's tier-5 MCP call from a relaunched process needs an admitted `restart` pool and waits on the gate.
- **Dial wording.** Do not write "the supervisor never dials a second time" or "retries within the adapter's wait". Retry only `ECONNREFUSED`; an accepted connection that fails the UID check exits, so a squatter gets no retries. CODE-4 does not render `--dial-timeout`, and the retry interval is an overridable non-spec constant.
- **Subject agreement.** Round 2's unconditional "The adapter has the runtime supervisor start the SDK process" gave the embedded model a supervisor (MISTAKE, one round lost). When rewording a sentence's timing or citation, check that its subject agrees with what the cited section assigns.
- **Moving lifetime content.** Grep spec/ for "Runtime process lifetime" before moving content out of that paragraph; §10.1 carried a retirement rule through that citation.
- **Launch wait is no barrier.** "Barrier lock" in spec/28 (§28.6, CH-CHECKPOINT, the CH-BARRIER row) means the checkpoint barrier; do not edit those sentences for the adapter's launch wait or cite them about launch serialization. Widen the reuse predicate only in **Launch.**.
- **SPEC-4 item 8 list.** **Where it runs.** lists every supervisor-originated message kind (`ready`, replies, exit reports) and is exhaustive by design; `ready` was once added without updating it. Add any new kind there.
- **Order-dependent sub-steps.** SPEC-12(b)'s citation list must not name a token that a later SPEC-12 sub-step's find string quotes; the sub-steps apply in order against the evolving text.
- **Grant follows the carrier.** The SPEC-13 RBAC cell and CODE-6's read mechanism must agree; changing the preflight result carrier changes the grant.
- **Registry critical section.** Any step the adapter performs under the slot-registry lock must be added to §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.**, which declares itself the only statement of that atomicity.
- **§4.7.1 insert mirrors the end events.** Whenever the **Runtime exits.** intro's list of end events changes, the SPEC-2(d) insert ("In the sidecar model, on an ...") must change with it. prune36 widened it to the connection end only and missed `ended`, which round 39 filed: the `deleting` row's drain `end` fails held sessions when `ended` is the first event, and that must be ordered against `Shutdown`'s deregistration by the lock (`[spec.39.review-reliability.1]`).
- **Claim pointer pair.** SPEC-5(i) points §5.2 **Runtime not live:** at the **Liveness.** table and the **Claim** bullet points back; the pair is deliberate (table: which path; §5.2: the trigger rule). Do not resolve the cycle by restating either side, and do not re-add "as the release of a refused or failed start does" clauses, which paraphrased §4.6.3's binding clause and drifted.
- **§17.4 heredoc schema.** The §17.4 `runtime-crds.yaml` heredoc that SPEC-1(d) edits already fails the Runtime CRD schema (no required `type`; `image: my-agent:dev` lacks the `@sha256:` digest). Both predate 0087; do not file them against SPEC-1(d).
- **Scrub release row citation.** The §5.2 scrub-table release row cites §4.7.1 for "deregisters ... before it writes `session_end`"; the ordering holds only with the §28.5.3 **Session frame writes.** Shutdown row read alongside. Judged not a false citation.
- **Held versus registered.** Do not write "the sessions whose entries are registered" for the sessions a process holds: an entry exists from rule 4 (`PrepareWorkspace`) and the hold from the rule-8 record. Rounds 32 and 33 created and removed that drift. **Crash.** carries no consequence clause: "one handled after it fails none" contradicted it on a concurrent pod (MISTAKE).
- **Scrub order prose.** Do not re-add restart-pod end, sweep, or rebind positions to the SPEC-5(c) order sentence; "runtime end and sweep" as plain prose elsewhere stays true. Add no bound value, error code, or `scrub_warning` path for a sweep bound-hit.
- **Signalled children.** A child ended by a signal has `WEXITSTATUS` 0, so a worker that tests only that reports a SIGKILLed command as success. The worker's own deadline SIGKILL is `cleanup_timeout`, never `cleanup_failed`. Fork keeps the worker non-dumpable and `execve` resets it for the commands; keep that split in any restatement of the keep-pod residue.
- **Step-0 gate placement.** The step-0 skip of `cleanupCommands` belongs in `scrub.Run` for every pod; a gate on the `CleanupRunner` path alone leaves unsupervised pods running deployer code after a failed credential purge, which today's `scrub.Run` already does.
- **No SIGTERM forward.** Do not restore a tini-style forward: the runtime is not its container's PID 1, so a forwarded SIGTERM kills a handlerless SDK runtime at T0 and fails sessions during the preStop eviction checkpoint. The tier-8 case "deleting a supervised pod sends its runtime process no signal before the adapter exits" discriminates. Round 10 wrongly called this fix outside the staged spec.
- **Adapter-initiated closes.** A close the adapter makes to end a generation (the hold timeout) selects its own row, never `none`; CODE-3 sets `disposition` under `s.mu` before `CloseListener`, and `holdTimeout` precedes `deleting`. A close as the adapter exits, or for the rebind, selects no row (Settled **Own closes**). A row that decides an outcome before the `none` row states the service-mode adapter exit itself.
- **Exit status meaning.** Do not restate the status-0, non-zero, result-delivered outcome at any proposal site; cite §28.5.3 **Exit Codes**. Exit Codes points to **Runtime exits.** for the outcome and the `shutdown` row points back for the status meaning; those are different contents. The code-0 row's "shutdown honored" is a meaning; do not file it.
- **Adapter exit is not a row.** Do not re-add an `adapter-exit` or `rebind` row or a Retirement column to **Runtime exits.**, do not restate §6.2 **Pod crash during an active session.** there, and do not edit the **Exit** channel's `reserved`/`claimed` sentence (it covers causes only). Tier 8 kills the adapter; the tier-1 own-close case asserts only that no handler runs.
- **§6.2 cause wording.** Name both containers wherever the **Exit** predicate does, because a runtime-container-only termination is reachable after READY. Do not restore precursor narration to SPEC-2(f); `[precursor-c29693b62.2026-10-22]`'s site list is stale for SPEC-2(f).
- **Withdrawn idle exit.** Do not reintroduce an exit predicate over the registry or session history: the registry is empty in the deregistration-to-scrub gap of every recycle (`answerShutdown`; the concurrent pod's two `Shutdown`s), and rounds 31 and 32 each found another window. Do not reopen how long a latched pod keeps its place.
- **Supervisor death versus freeze.** The supervisor is PID 1 of the runtime container, so an outcome that ends it terminates the container and the WarmPoolController sees that ("another path can retire the pod first"). SIGSTOP and protocol link failures leave the container running, and only **Start** retires such a pod. Close any frozen-supervisor gap with a reclaimer, never by gating on init immunity (D-PROPERTIES).
- **Do-not-refile list.** Settled and declined repeatedly: agent-pod `restartPolicy: Never` stated only in the card; the §13.2 label note (non-exhaustive; the `dns-policy` mismatch is pre-existing and recorded; do not add `runtime-process`); §26.3 steps 4 to 6 "adapter" (recorded defect; "platform adapter" is undefined in spec/); §26.4 to §26.6 "Entrypoint:" lines and the §26.7 chat "highlights" YAML; the §6.1 row "The SDK is re-warmed at the next recycle boundary."; §6.1 `sdk_connecting` force-terminate; §15.4.2 DRAINING and §15.4.6 drain rows; §10.1 "Before exiting" (the adapter does not exit); keep-pod cleanup background processes (D-CLEANUP); keep-pod "a cleanup it disrupts fails the scrub"; §24.2 "two execution modes"; the WarmPoolController creating no pod with no condition; the `none` row's unstated line limit (tree: `maxJSONLFrameBytes`, 50 MB); §16 warm-up failure and §17.7 runbook cause lists (docs-only); SPEC-5(e) dropping "Steps 3 and 5 act on the adapter's own environment"; §5.1 "mirrors only `restartable`" (read in its paragraph; the reconciler also mirrors `requireSoPeercred`); SPEC-5(e) "the runtime end"; the supervisor's launch environment arriving in argv.
- **Declined in round 39.** Rejected with reasons; do not refile: §6.2 **Pod crash during an active session.** as the wrong home for an adapter exit at the end of termination (reopens the round-38 prune; same recovery path); a missing edge row for the D-CLEANUP `setsid` bound (settled); the `deleting` row's drain `end` not being a launch point **End.** lists (an example; R16's write); SPEC-1(b) "Forbidden wherever `command` is." (wording only); SPEC-5(b) omitting the PSC copy into `SandboxTemplate.spec` (transport of the value); the WarmPoolController creating no pod for a no-longer-`restartable` runtime without a status condition (hardening); the start-versus-end race at the rule-8 record (Settled **Start refusal atomicity**); item 8's "no author-controlled byte" against the `exited` status (strictly decoded, decides nothing); the hold-timeout pod with no reclaimer when no coordinator returns (pre-existing; **Claim** covers any release); the `shutdown` row swallowing a crash that races the write (R16); the hold-timeout "no connection" double mention and the scrub table's "launched error fails the start" against card **Degradation.** (one-clause citations); the §24 intro describing `lenny-ctl preflight` as two-mode (the "two execution modes" item above).
- **Security reasoning not to redo.** Nonce-only `CH-SUPERVISE` squatting yields denial of service rather than a forged end, because the adapter closes its listener after the first accept. A runtime can steer a `CH-MSGSOCK` end only toward a row that sets not-live; `endDemote`, `endScrub`, `shutdown`, `holdTimeout`, and `deleting` each need a trusted act. 0096 must not read the reduced `allowCrossTenantReuse` text as sufficient on a `keep` pod.
- **Sequence time budget.** If the missing-report grace is shorter than two ends and two sweeps on a loaded node, `restart` pods retire through the timeout; the code loop sizes request deadlines against it.
- **Pre-READY wait covers image pull.** The kubelet pulls the runtime image after the adapter starts, inside the adapter's wait for `ready`, and no controller warming deadline exists; a wait shorter than a cold pull fails the pod (filed against SPEC-7 **Timing.** in round 19, judged tuning in round 28).
- **Pre-existing mismatches to leave.** The §15.4 demotion contract says 10 s while `ConfigureWorkspace` and `DefaultDemoteTimeout` use 5 s; the §5.1 `cleanupCommands` example `rm -rf /tmp/sandbox-*` needs a shell glob the argv runner never expands; the §28 lead-in "exits non-zero without emitting a `response`" is narrower than the Crash column. File each separately if raised.
- **Wire contract keys.** The card names `type`, `gen`, `id`, `error`, `graceMs`, and `properties`; strict decoding makes every member a contract between the C supervisor, the Go adapter, and `lenny-ctl preflight`.
- **Tier-8 signal case on runc.** SIGKILL and SIGSTOP from the probe do not retire a pod on runc, where namespace init is immune; tier 8 asserts the supervisor keeps answering `ping`, and the retirement case is tier 6.
- **Gate rework.** With D-RESTARTGATE in force, D-PREREQS and the checklist's "before proposal 0084" line need rework unless `restart` admission is split from the spec steps (`[f4.human-decisions]`).

(3) §17.8.1 and §17.8.2 do not list the new controller timing settings that CODE-4 adds as `controller.tuning` knobs (`[non-spec.6.review-single-source.1]`, `[non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]`, `[prune-remedy.2026-10-23.1]`, `[prune-remedy.2026-10-23.7]`).
- **Next non-spec sweep agenda (OPEN).** The next full sweep takes each of these as an agenda item and either stages it or records why not: (1) CODE-5 does not state that pool admission rejects an unknown `recycle.runtimeProcess` value, and no tier-1 case names one; (2) the CODE-3 scrub-deadline IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE requires the deadline to fit inside the gateway's missing-report grace, which the adapter cannot know; (3) **Lifetime code comments** above; (4) the gcp and azure e2e source of the supervisor image, since only aws has a build script (`[prune-remedy.2026-10-23.1]`).

### Deferred

- DEFERRED [pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json, through non-spec-changes.md schema section] (`[spec.8.review-client-surface.1]`, `[spec.10.review-client-surface.1]`, `[spec.16.review-client-surface.1]`, `[spec.38.review-client-surface.1]`): the `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` description says the field is required on every reusing recycling pool "because the pod then keeps one runtime process across its sessions". After SPEC-5(g) that is false for a `restart` pod: the field is required only under `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep`. The non-spec staging names only the new `runtimeProcess` property for openapi.json; it should also rewrite this description. The served `residualStateWarning` has no description, so only the acknowledgment text changes.
- DEFERRED [proposals/0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7/0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.problem-statement.md] (`[spec.1.fix-G6.1]`): line 25, "Every sidecar Runtime declares `command`, including through the admin registration API", records the plan's direction, which owner-approved D-COMMAND departs from. The pass between loops should confirm it reads as plan history rather than the staged design.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md DOC-1] (`[spec.8.follow-up-fix.1]`): DOC-1 stages no site for the client docs that mirror the reworded §5.2 **Client visibility of weak-isolation reuse.** sentence and the §7.1 `residualStateWarning` row (SPEC-5(h)). `docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md` (the `residualStateWarning` row at :418 and the paragraph after the table at :421) and `docs/api/mcp.md` (the `sessionIsolationLevel` paragraph at :160) say "On a recycling pool a session may run in a runtime process that served an earlier session of the same tenant"; true after SPEC-5(h) is "On a pod that keeps its runtime process (`recycle.runtimeProcess: keep`) a session may run in a runtime process that served an earlier session of the same tenant". (`[spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]` judged "may run" still true at the client's level of knowledge; the docs loop decides.)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md DOC-1] (`[spec.8.review-docs-alignment.1]`, `[spec.9.review-docs-alignment.1]`): docs pages state the kept-process posture by pool and need `keep`-pod wording: docs/operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md:64 and docs/operator-guide/namespace-and-isolation.md:261 ("because a recycled pod keeps the runtime process that served its pinned tenant", which SPEC-5 rewrites in §5.2 to "because the tenant pin applies on every pool"), docs/operator-guide/configuration.md:305, and the client pages in the entry above. None is in the DOC-1 list.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md DOC-1] (`[spec.10.review-docs-alignment.1]`): docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md documents `lenny-ctl preflight` but not the new `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor --runtime-class` command that SPEC-13 adds to §24.2; DOC-1 names only docs/operator-guide/security.md for it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
FACT: Store validators take only the Pool: `ValidateSessionPolicy(p Pool)` and `validateRecyclePolicy(p Pool, r *RecyclePolicy)`; "the pure poolstore record carries no runtime". Runtime-dependent admission checks run in the admin create/PUT handlers and the bootstrap seed after `r.runtimes.Get`, beside `ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/poolstore.go:552, :599, :688, :724; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:604-628, :1650-1665; bootstrap_resources.go:47-62
DECISION: the `restartable` refusal is a new runtime-taking validator `poolstore.ValidateRuntimeProcessRestartable(restartable bool, p Pool)` called beside `ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode` in create, PUT (dry runs included), and the seed; unresolved runtime reads as not restartable; the D-RESTARTGATE gate stays in `validateRecyclePolicy` — BECAUSE the store path cannot see a runtime and the gate must still hold there — ALTERNATIVES: threading the runtime into the store interface (both stores change for one flag); moving the gate to the handlers (loses store-path refusal; settled).
WATCHOUT: the `restartable` check reads the runtime `runtimestore.Resolve` returns, while the handlers today call `r.runtimes.Get`; a derived runtime's stored record lacks the base's `Restartable`, so an implementor reusing the handler's existing `rt` gets a wrong answer for derived runtimes. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/merge.go:16
DECISION: a NotFound pod read in `rebindReserved` skips the candidate — BECAUSE copying `AdmitTenantPin`'s NotFound-admits branch would rebind onto a vanished pod, and failing the acquisition discards remaining candidates — ALTERNATIVES: admit (rebinds a dead pod), error (fails whole acquisition).
CORRECTS [Standing context "Restart gate (D-RESTARTGATE)"]: "after the `restartable` refusal" holds only on paths that resolve the runtime; on the store's Create/Update path only the gate runs. The summary's D-RESTARTGATE no longer cites "§29.2 step 26a" (SPEC-12(b) renumbers it to 27a); it cites §29.2 without a step.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G6.1]
FACT: No agent-pod container has a readiness, startup, or liveness probe; the sandbox-ready gate is set by `syncReadinessGate` only when the Pod's `ContainersReady` is true, and never cleared — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `syncReadinessGate`; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go adapter container literal (port named `grpc`); base_pod.go RestartPolicyNever
FACT: The adapter binds its listener with `net.Listen("tcp", *addr)` after `PeercredSelftest` and immediately before `srv.Serve(lis)`; the gRPC server is mTLS in production — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go (PeercredSelftest, net.Listen, srv.Serve; header comment on --tls-* flags)
DECISION: READY reaches Pod readiness through a `tcpSocket` readiness probe on the adapter `grpc` port of every sidecar pod (CODE-4), with CODE-3 ordering `AwaitSupervisor` and the warm-up launch before `net.Listen` — BECAUSE agent pods have no RBAC, so kubelet readiness is the only carrier to the controller's gate, and a bind needs no new adapter state — ALTERNATIVES: grpc probe (kubelet gRPC probes do not speak TLS), exec probe with marker file (new subcommand and state on distroless read-only fs), startup/liveness probe (kills the container, failing the pod under restartPolicy Never during a cold pull), adapter/gateway writing the condition (no RBAC; controller owns it)
WATCHOUT: Do not move `net.Listen` earlier than the pre-READY gates in cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go, or the pod becomes claimable before the supervisor's `ready`; TEST-1 Tier 5 "the pod reports Ready only after the supervisor connects" is the discriminating assertion — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 main.go bullet, CODE-4 probe bullet
FACT: G4 had already merged its `--adapter-uid` case into the TEST-1 Tier 1 pod-builder item (now "the pod builder's `env` and platform `--env` rendering order, ..."), so the design's anchor "the pod builder;" no longer exists; the probe case was inserted into that item after the `--adapter-uid` clause.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: poolstore's pool validators reached from the store (`ValidateSessionPolicy` -> `validateRecyclePolicy`, called by memory `Create`/`Update` and pgstore) take only the Pool; every runtime-dependent admission check (`ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode`, `ValidateCrossTenantReuseTier`, `ValidateNonceOnlyAcknowledgment`) is called from the admin create handler, `validatePoolUpdate` (PUT and PUT dry run, effective post-update runtimeRef), and the bootstrap seed after `r.runtimes.Get`, before `validatePoolForStore`/store `Create`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/poolstore.go:552,599,688,724,871,969; pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:80; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:604-662,1497-1507,1640-1684; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go:47-66
DECISION: the `restartable` refusal moves to a new runtime-taking poolstore validator called beside `ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode`; the D-RESTARTGATE gate stays in `validateRecyclePolicy` (it needs no runtime and must hold on the store path) — BECAUSE the restartable check cannot be computed where the store calls validators — ALTERNATIVES: threading a runtime into `ValidateSessionPolicy`/store `Create` (changes the store interface for one flag; the store has no runtime store), moving the gate to the handlers too (loses the store-path refusal).
WATCHOUT: admin handlers use `r.runtimes.Get`, not `runtimestore.Resolve`; a derived runtime's stored record does not carry the base's `Restartable` (CODE-1 `Merge` does), so the new check must resolve. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:604; pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/merge.go:16,55
WATCHOUT: `rebindReserved` pod NotFound outcome was unspecified (CODE-5 named only the non-NotFound error); `AdmitTenantPin` admits on NotFound (tenantpin.go:53), which would rebind onto a vanished pod if copied. Design picks skip-the-candidate. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/tenantpin.go:52-56; claimer.go:302-345
CORRECTS [Standing context "Restart gate (D-RESTARTGATE)"]: the `restartable` refusal is not in `validateRecyclePolicy`; it is a runtime-taking validator in the handlers and seed, and "after the `restartable` refusal" holds only on paths that resolve the runtime (a direct store Create sees only the interim gate).
CORRECTS [Standing context "MCP nonce belongs to 0084"]: "§29.2 step 26a" becomes step 27a once SPEC-12(b) lands; staged text now cites §29.2 without a step number.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: The fail-closed refusal of a `type: agent` sidecar Runtime with no `command` lives in `createPod` (beside the `restartable` refusal), and `Build` keeps only the empty-`SupervisorImage` refusal — BECAUSE `podspec.Inputs` carries no runtime type and `createPod` already holds the Runtime CRD — ALTERNATIVES: add `RuntimeType` to `Inputs` (a field whose only use is a check createPod can make with the CRD in hand); delete the guard and rely on CEL (a CRD upgrade does not revalidate stored Runtime objects, so a long-lived Kind cluster would silently run an unsupervised agent pod).
FACT: `podspec.Inputs` has `DeploymentModel` but no runtime type; `createPod` reads `rt.Spec` directly — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:83-118, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:384-400
DECISION: Fixture/test migrations live in the step whose check breaks them: `command` (and image `ENV` as `env`) on every sidecar agent Runtime a test creates goes in CODE-1 (S14), `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep` on every admitted reusing recycling pool goes in CODE-5 (S17) — BECAUSE S14 runs tier 2 and S17 runs tier 4, so deferring to FIXTURE-1 (S20) leaves those steps red — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's FIXTURE-1 placement (breaks S14/S17); enumerating every file (stale on the next test added).
WATCHOUT: `pkg/embedded/stack/runtimeapply.go` defaults an absent `type` to `agent` and an absent `deploymentModel` to `sidecar`, so Runtime CRDs that set neither (tests/tier2_component/controllers/embedded_runtime_apply_test.go, pkg/embedded/stack/runtimeapply_envtest_test.go, tests/tier4_integration/embedded_mode_smoke_test.go) are hit by the CEL rule too — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/runtimeapply.go:264-275
WATCHOUT: The supervisor ignores the image ENTRYPOINT, CMD, and ENV, so a fixture's `command` must be the full argv the image's ENTRYPOINT plus CMD gave, and its `env` the image ENV it relied on.
FACT: `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml` is the `make generate` render of the chart under charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml and includes the reference-runtime Runtime CRDs and the controller args; tier-11 TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender fails on drift — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:1-5, :3899, :4923-4940; Makefile:62,74
FACT: `lenny up` imports only PLATFORM_BUNDLE_IMAGES (gateway, controller, ops, adapter) — EVIDENCE: Makefile:223-233

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: the supervisor's `--adapter-uid` is optional and CODE-4 renders it on exactly the pods where `nonceOnlyArgs` renders no flag (one predicate, `Inputs.RequireSoPeercred`) — BECAUSE staged SPEC-7 **Endpoint.** conditions the supervisor's check on "When `SO_PEERCRED` is enforced" and D-AUTH says nonce-only uses neither check — ALTERNATIVES: a separate `--no-peer-check` flag (second spelling of one decision); a sentinel UID value (a magic value the C parser must special-case).
DECISION: the supervisor-volume mount rule needs the webhook translation: `podsecurity.VolumeMount.ReadOnly`, `ContainerSpec.Init`, and the volume name passed into `webhook.PodSecurity` from `cmd/lenny-webhook` as `podspec.CredVolumeName` already is — BECAUSE `translateContainer` copies only Name and MountPath — ALTERNATIVES: a podsecurity-package constant for the volume name (breaks the CredVolumeName precedent and makes podsecurity know a podspec name).
DECISION: the preflight supervisor pod reads its namespace, UIDs, cred GID, stage UID, and supervisor image from the `--config` values file the existing `lenny-ctl preflight` already takes, and runs at the stage UID — BECAUSE the SPEC-13 RBAC cell grants only pods and pods/log, so reading the controller Deployment is out — ALTERNATIVES: separate flags per value (four new flags); container named `runtime` at the agent UID (passes identity, but the kill-reach and non-dumpable properties do not depend on the UID).
FACT: `lenny-pod-security` has no objectSelector: every pod CREATE in a namespace labelled `lenny.dev/agent-namespace=true` is validated, so any one-off pod there must carry fsGroup and supplementalGroups equal to the cred-readers GID even though it mounts no credential. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/admission-policies/pod-security-webhook.yaml:41-45; pkg/podsecurity/podsecurity.go:262-282
FACT: `webhook.PodSecurity` callers are cmd/lenny-webhook/main.go:192, five sites in pkg/admission/webhook/pod_security_test.go, and pod_security_uid_lockstep_test.go:84; nothing else builds `podsecurity.VolumeMount`. EVIDENCE: grep over pkg cmd tests
WATCHOUT: CODE-6 must wait for the preflight pod's completion by polling `get`, never `watch`/`list`, because the SPEC-13 RBAC cell grants create, get, delete on pods and get on pods/log only (Settled trap **Grant follows the carrier.**). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:1076
WATCHOUT: a restartable init container (`restartPolicy: Always`) NAMED `lenny-supervisor-stage` would keep a read-write mount for the pod's life; the rule as staged admits it by name. Only a non-controller pod creator can produce one. OPEN for a later security round, not filed here.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md SPEC-13]: the §24.2 row `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor --runtime-class <name>` names no source for the agent namespace, UIDs, cred GID, or supervisor image; the command needs `--config <values.yaml>` as `lenny-ctl preflight --config` already has (spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:43). The row should read `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor --runtime-class <name> --config <values.yaml>`.

WATCHOUT: F-4.7.26 must stay OPEN until S18 deletes the rows; implement-proposal closes referenced findings after the last step, so closing it at S16 turns tier 0 red. EVIDENCE: validate-maps "blocker does not resolve to an open item".

EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/deployment_boundary_render_test.go attribute, buildCases, renderCases; tests/tier0_static/deployment_boundary_test.go uncoveredInputsFields, reverseEnvDefects.
DECISION: The supervisor's argv (a C binary with no Go flag set) is checked by running it: TEST-1's tier-3 contract test stages and starts the supervisor from the argv podspec.Build renders; the tier-0 gate maps the two sites and keeps I5's sidecar-runtime variable rule on the platform `--env` pairs — BECAUSE it puts the "binary accepts its rendered argv" check where the real binary runs, with no Go mirror of the C parser to drift — ALTERNATIVES: a Go flag-set mirror of the C parser in the gate (drifts silently from supervisor.c); a podspec-exported flag list shared by renderer and gate (tautological, checks nothing); scanning supervisor.c for flag literals (fragile, new scanner).
WATCHOUT: Moving LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET from the runtime container's Env into `--env` argv empties the only input I5's sidecar-runtime rule reads, so without the retarget the rule passes vacuously. EVIDENCE: deployment_boundary_test.go reverseEnvDefects.

Separate finding for a later round.
OPEN: The WIRED claim row "Adapter-to-runtime heartbeat with SIGTERM escalation" may need its surface retargeted (SURFACE_OVERRIDES) if CODE-3 changes how heartbeat.go escalates on a supervised pod; nobody checked.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
FACT: No agent-pod container carries any probe today; `syncReadinessGate` sets `lenny.dev/sandbox-ready` once `ContainersReady` is True and never clears it, so without a probe a sidecar pod goes `warming -> idle` (or ends warm-fill `sdk_connecting`) as soon as its containers start, before the adapter serves. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `syncReadinessGate`; pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go `Decide`/`DecideSDKWarm` `case PodReady`; grep ReadinessProbe pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec (no hit).
FACT: The adapter binds its gRPC listener (`net.Listen("tcp", *addr)`) immediately before `srv.Serve`, after `PeercredSelftest`; the gRPC server is mTLS in production and also serves the standard gRPC health service. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go header comment, `net.Listen` and `srv.Serve` at the end of `main`.
DECISION: READY reaches Pod readiness through a `tcpSocket` readiness probe on the adapter container's `grpc` port, rendered by the sidecar builder on every sidecar pod; CODE-3 orders `AwaitSupervisor` and the warm-up launch before `net.Listen`, so the probe passes only after every pre-READY gate. No controller or lifecycle change: the existing gate keyed on `ContainersReady` carries it — BECAUSE it is the only carrier compatible with zero agent-pod RBAC and needs no new adapter state — ALTERNATIVES: `grpc` probe on the health service (kubelet gRPC probes do not speak TLS, so it fails on every mTLS pod); exec probe of an adapter subcommand reading a marker file (new subcommand, new marker state, a Go process per period in a distroless read-only container); adapter writes the gate itself (agent pods have no apiserver path); startup or liveness probe (a failure kills the container, and under `restartPolicy: Never` fails the pod during a long cold image pull that the pre-READY wait is meant to absorb).
DECISION: no spec staging. §6.2 already states the warm-fill edge as "readiness gate set" and §4.7.9 step 4 as "signals READY"; the probe is the code that makes both true. §29's note that no channel row carries READY stays a separate spec gap.
WATCHOUT: idle with `PodNotReady` is "leave it warm" in `Decide`, and the sandbox-ready gate is never cleared, so a transient probe failure after READY neither retires nor un-idles a pod; the gateway's re-warm poll (`recycleboundary.go`) reads Pod Ready and waits through such a blip. Do not add a not-ready arm to `Decide` as part of this fix.
WATCHOUT: the probe timing values are not spec-fixed; code-best-practices requires them overridable. The implementor owns that; the proposal does not name values.
OPEN: embedded pods have the same missing carrier (their pre-READY `SO_PEERCRED` self-test and any embedded pre-connect also precede `srv.Serve` with no probe). Outside D-SCOPE; a separate finding for a later round or a direct fix.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 WATCHOUT "`SDKWarmReady` ... the READY gate is the adapter's `srv.Serve` ordering, not a readiness-probe hook"]: the `srv.Serve` ordering alone reaches nothing outside the adapter; after this fix the gate is that ordering carried to Pod readiness by the adapter's readiness probe. `SDKWarmReady` still has no caller and stays unused.
USEFUL [Warm-fill causes precede READY]: that standing entry is true only once this probe lands; it was the lead that showed the claim depended on an unstaged carrier.

DECISION: the CODE-3 sentence "`launch` resets `disposition` to `none`, so a generation launched after ... both fail the same sessions ..." is deleted outright, not replaced — BECAUSE the spec intro's "Each launch sets the value to `none`" is the rule and the rest is a defence; the Adapter state `Cleared` cell `launch` carries the code mapping.

The srv.Serve = READY mapping survives in the flow diagram (non-spec-changes.md:30), so deleting "Before `srv.Serve`" loses nothing.

WATCHOUT: DOC-1 lifetime statements must carry the `DemoteSDK` exception of SPEC-2(a) (Standing Trap **Unconditional lifetime claims**); the design puts that rule once in the DOC-1 preamble, and each page bullet only names its site.
FACT: the only tier-11 test that requires a lifetime phrase is `TestRuntimeProcessLifetimeNamesTheAdapterClosesOfTheConnection` — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/runtime_session_end_paths_doc_test.go:71-77 (requires "is not re-created or reconnected inside the pod" and "the pod is retired").
FACT: today `scrub.Run` runs `runCleanup` unconditionally after step 0 — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go:248-258; `DefaultOps` steps 1/1b exec `sh`/`ipcrm` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go:36-46; defaultops_test.go has no kill or IPC test.
USEFUL [Standing Traps **Step-0 gate placement**]: confirmed the missing unsupervised-pod test is the discriminating case for the gate.

OPEN (separate finding): summary.md risks bullet "The precursor fix on `fix/warmpool-reclaim-terminated-adapter` must land before S1 ..." restates D-PREREQS's deletion-timestamp behavior; its tier-8 assertion note ("assert the pod's deletion and its replacement rather than the intermediate phase") belongs in TEST-1 **Tier 8.**.

### [non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The lenny-pod-security webhook does not pass a mount's read-only flag to the validator. `translateContainer` copies only `Name` and `MountPath` into `podsecurity.VolumeMount`, and it puts init, regular, and ephemeral containers into one `Containers` list that has no container-kind field. A read-write-mount rule therefore needs `pkg/admission/webhook/pod_security.go` changed as well as `pkg/podsecurity`. EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/webhook/pod_security.go:112-127, :154-156; pkg/podsecurity/podsecurity.go:182-185
FACT: `ValidateAgentPod` applies to every pod in a namespace labelled `lenny.dev/agent-namespace=true`, the CODE-6 preflight pod included. It requires fsGroup to equal the lenny-cred-readers GID, supplementalGroups to contain that GID, runAsNonRoot, and a non-zero, non-reserved container runAsUser. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/admission-policies/pod-security-webhook.yaml:40-45; pkg/podsecurity/podsecurity.go:263, :282
FACT: podspec `Inputs` already carries `PreConnect`, so the CODE-4 render-time `restartable` guard can read it. This closes the Open entry **podspec Inputs and preConnect**. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go (createPod, `PreConnect: rt.Spec.Capabilities != nil && ...`)
DECISION: I filed CODE-6 for having no test and no admission constraint, which turns the Open entry **Preflight pod admission** into a finding — BECAUSE the fail-closed webhook refuses a pod that lacks the cred-GID fsGroup — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it Open (rejected: verified against the code).

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: No agent-pod container carries a readiness, startup, or liveness probe (grep of pkg/ and cmd/ for ReadinessProbe/StartupProbe/LivenessProbe finds none outside the gateway's own startup TLS probe), and the WarmPoolController sets the `lenny.dev/sandbox-ready` gate as soon as ContainersReady is true (pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:728-750). ### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 installs the existing SIGTERM handler (`signal.Notify`, then `ExitOnSignal`) before `AwaitSupervisor`, runs the pre-READY gates under a SIGTERM-cancelled context, and sets `sdkConnected` when the warm-up `launch` is sent — BECAUSE the tree installs the handler after `net.Listen`, so a warm-fill `sdk_connecting` deletion would die by Go's default disposition and skip §6.1 **Adapter SIGTERM behavior during `sdk_connecting`.** — ALTERNATIVES: uncancelled gates (EndProcess contends with the launch mutex, main binds after shutdown); default disposition (abrupt supervisor kill skips the bounded `end`); gates after `net.Listen` plus a readiness flag (reopens the tcpSocket probe decision).
WATCHOUT: tree `Server.PreConnect` sets `sdkConnected` only after a successful connect, and `ShutdownDemoteSDK` returns early when it is false, so a SIGTERM during an in-flight warm-up launch would send no `end` unless the flag is set at `launch` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go `PreConnect` and `ShutdownDemoteSDK`; cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go `net.Listen` precedes `signal.Notify`.
WATCHOUT: the deletion-during-warm-up cluster case lives in TEST-1 Tier 5 (same delayed-`CH-MSGSOCK`-dial probe knob as the READY-after-warm-up case); do not add a Tier 8 twin.
OPEN: TEST-1 Tier 8 "deleting a supervised pod sends its runtime process no signal before the adapter exits" is false as worded for a `preConnect` pod with `sdkConnected` set, where `ExitOnSignal` sends `end` before exit; it needs scoping to the kubelet signal (separate finding, not edited here).

DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 1 D-RESTARTGATE clause names the bootstrap seed as a test subject with its own outcome (per-entry `error` with code `SEED_VALIDATION`) — BECAUSE without it a missing or misordered `ValidateRuntimeProcessRestartable` call in the seed is masked by the interim message — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "seed returns 400 VALIDATION_ERROR" (false for the seed); a separate seed bullet (restates both outcomes).
FACT: the bootstrap seed refuses a pool as a per-entry `actionError` with `seedValidationCode` ("SEED_VALIDATION") and answers 207 Multi-Status on partial failure, never 400 — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go (ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode block), pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap.go:42, :255-258
WATCHOUT: do not put `SEED_VALIDATION` in staged spec text; it is a tree code absent from spec/.
