### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 main.go bullet installs the SIGTERM handler before `AwaitSupervisor`, runs the pre-READY gates under a context SIGTERM cancels, and a SIGTERM there runs `ExitOnSignal` (whose `ShutdownDemoteSDK` ends a launched warm-up process, connected or not) and exits without binding the listener — BECAUSE it keeps the round-1 probe/ordering design and only moves the handler; §6.1 **Adapter SIGTERM behavior during `sdk_connecting`.** is the requirement and is cited, not restated — ALTERNATIVES: handler installed early but gates not cancelled (EndProcess contends with the launch mutex held through the connect wait, ShutdownDemoteSDK overruns to ForceTerminate, and main then binds the listener anyway); keep Go default disposition and rely on the supervisor killing the runtime when the adapter's connection closes (violates §6.1 step 1); move the gates after `net.Listen` with an adapter readiness flag (reopens the round-1 probe decision, review-log DECISION at line 524).
WATCHOUT: tree `Server.PreConnect` sets `sdkConnected` only after `sw.PreConnect` returns success, and `ShutdownDemoteSDK` returns early when it is false, so a cancelled warm-up sends no `end` unless `sdkConnected` is set when `launch` is sent — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:152-170, :78-80
FACT: the only SIGTERM handler is the `signal.Notify` goroutine installed after `net.Listen`; before it Go's default disposition kills the adapter — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:468-484; preStop signals PID 1 (the adapter) — pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go:139
DECISION: the service-mode half of finding 1 needs no fix — BECAUSE §6.1's DemoteSDK requirement is scoped to `sdk_connecting`, which a service-mode pod never enters, and `ShutdownDemoteSDK` does nothing on a non-preConnect pod after READY either, so deletion during its warm-up behaves as deletion of an idle service-mode pod.
DECISION: both cluster cases go in TEST-1 Tier 5 behind one probe knob (the probe delays its `CH-MSGSOCK` dial), rather than a Tier 8 case — BECAUSE pod deletion is Tier-5 lifecycle and the delay makes both the READY window and the deletion window deterministic.
OPEN: separate finding — TEST-1 Tier 8 "deleting a supervised pod sends its runtime process no signal before the adapter exits" is false on a `preConnect` pod whose `sdkConnected` is set, because `ExitOnSignal` → `ShutdownDemoteSDK` sends `end` before the adapter exits; the case needs scoping to the kubelet signal (the **Signals.** kubelet row) or to non-preConnect pools.

### [non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: The CODE-4 readiness-probe mechanism holds end to end. `observePod` maps a Running pod to PodReady only when the Pod's Ready condition is True. `syncReadinessGate` flips `lenny.dev/sandbox-ready` only after `ContainersReady`. `Decide` takes `warming → idle` only on PodReady and leaves an `idle` pod that turns NotReady alone. So an adapter `tcpSocket` probe gated on `net.Listen` keeps the pod out of `idle` until READY, and a later probe flap retires nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:255-283, :727-750; pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:75-97
FACT: Directly applying a SandboxTemplate cannot get around the D-RESTARTGATE pool-admission gate. Rule set 2 of `lenny-pool-config-validator` refuses any SandboxTemplate spec write from a principal other than the PoolScalingController SA. EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator.go:72-80 (ReasonUnauthorizedPoolConfigWrite), :204
FACT: Pool admission handlers resolve runtimes with `r.runtimes.Get` (raw row), not `runtimestore.Resolve`. CODE-5's `ValidateRuntimeProcessRestartable` must use `runtimestore.Resolve` (merge.go:16), as the proposal states, or a derived runtime reads restartable=false. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:605, :1650; bootstrap_resources.go:50
FACT: `runtime_definitions` also has a `session_policy` JSONB column. The admin runtime PUT copies `body.SessionPolicy` without calling `validateRecyclePolicy`. A runtime-level `recycle.runtimeProcess` therefore never reaches a pod, because pods take their lifetime from the pool's SandboxTemplate mirror. EVIDENCE: migrations/0167_runtime_definitions_execution_mode_service.up.sql:96-99; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/runtimes.go:1052-1054
WATCHOUT: `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` installs its SIGTERM handler only after `net.Listen`. After CODE-3, a pod deleted during AwaitSupervisor or the warm-up launch takes the Go default SIGTERM exit. The supervisor then exits on EOF. This is harmless, because the SDK still ends, but a tier-8 case that expects the §6.1 demote-timeout teardown before READY would observe an immediate exit instead. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:468-485
USEFUL [non-spec.r1 history]: the refuted list kept me from re-filing docs-mirror gaps such as the configuration.md and execution-modes.md YAML examples that lack `runtimeProcess`.

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]

EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:468-482; pkg/adapter/exit.go:29-36; pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go:139
FACT: `DecideSDKWarm` moves `warming -> sdk_connecting` as soon as the pod is Running and not Ready, so the warm-fill `sdk_connecting` window is the window before the adapter's readiness. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:192-197

A pod deleted during the warm-up launch then gets the Go default exit instead of the section 6.1 DemoteSDK sequence. Whoever fixes this must install the handler before the pre-READY gates, not move `net.Listen` back. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:127
FACT: Kubelet readiness probes are not blocked by NetworkPolicy, because node-to-pod traffic is always allowed, so the CODE-4 tcpSocket probe does not need an ingress row.

DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 8's pod-deletion clause changes its observable rather than its pod scope: it becomes "deleting a supervised pod whose probe image's `STOPSIGNAL` is SIGINT delivers no SIGINT to its runtime process (the kubelet row of §4.7.10 **Signals.**; SIGINT discriminates because `end` sends SIGTERM)", and the default-stop-signal half is dropped — BECAUSE the kubelet row (spec-changes.md:482) says the supervisor forwards nothing and the runtime runs on "until the adapter ends it with `end`"; "no signal before the adapter exits" asserts the absence of `end`, which the row never promises on any pod (preConnect `ShutdownDemoteSDK`, and any adapter drain `end`), while SIGINT reaches the runtime only by forwarding — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "that is not `preConnect`" (narrows a false universal; still false wherever the adapter's drain sends `end`, the `deleting` disposition rows); keeping the SIGTERM case with a timing assertion before the adapter's `end` (needs an observable of when `end` is sent; the C harness out-of-namespace sender case and Tier 9 already pin SIGTERM as a no-op); deleting the clause outright (loses the only cluster check that the image STOPSIGNAL reaches PID 1 and is ignored).
FACT: the kubelet sends the stop signal to the runtime container's init (the supervisor) only; `end` is SIGTERM then SIGKILL, and the supervisor's exit ends the rest by SIGKILL, so no platform path sends the runtime SIGINT — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:482-483, :491

WATCHOUT: do not re-add "with the default stop signal" to the Tier 8 case: under SIGTERM a forwarded signal and the `end` signal are the same signal, so the case cannot discriminate.

### [redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.1]
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): CODE-3 now carries a **Pre-READY sequence** table (rows P0 before `signal.Notify`, P1 `PeercredSelftest` and construction, P2 `AwaitSupervisor`, P3 warm-up `launch` sent and not connected, P4 warm-up connected, P5 `net.Listen` and `srv.Serve`; columns SIGTERM or SIGINT, gate failure, gate timeout, success), and the CODE-3 SDK-warm, service-mode, and main.go bullets, the deleted Warm-up bullet, the CODE-4 probe bullet, and the TEST-1 Tier 1, 5, and 8 clauses point at it — BECAUSE the pre-READY order and exits were restated at six sites, and the round-3 main.go bullet let a SIGTERM-cancelled gate exit non-zero while `ExitOnSignal` was still ending the process (`[non-spec.3.review-reliability.1]`) — ALTERNATIVES: patching the main.go bullet alone (leaves the restatements that produced the finding).
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): the exit race is resolved by an exit claim, one compare-and-swap on a phase word (`preReady` to `failed` or `ready` by `main`; `preReady` or `ready` to `signalled` by the handler). `main` is the only goroutine that ends the process: it exits 1 when it wins `failed`, and otherwise waits for `exitDone`, which the handler closes after `ExitOnSignal`, and returns 0 without binding the listener. `signal.Notify` moves to just after the `prestop` dispatch, and the sequence moves into `pkg/adapter/preready.go` (`RunPreReady`) with the signal channel, listen function, and exit function injected, so tier 1 pins every cell — BECAUSE it gives each cell one exit owner and makes the end complete before the exit; owner preference for simple designs — ALTERNATIVES: having `main` check `ctx.Err()` on a gate error (still races a signal that arrives after the check); having the handler call `os.Exit` (two exit paths, and deferred cleanup skipped).
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): a gate failure or timeout sends no `end`; the launched process ends when the adapter's exit closes `CH-SUPERVISE` and the supervisor exits (staged card **Degradation.** last sentence). Only the SIGTERM cells send `end` (`ShutdownDemoteSDK` on a `preConnect` pod, a new `EndProcess` branch of `ExitOnSignal` on a service-mode pod) — BECAUSE §6.1 governs the SIGTERM path only, and an `end` after a link failure cannot succeed — ALTERNATIVES: `EndProcess` before every non-zero exit (fails on the link-failure cells and delays the exit).
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): the ping goroutine starts when `AwaitSupervisor` returns, so a stopped supervisor during the warm-up is a P3 link failure; FIXTURE-1's probe gains a `CH-MSGSOCK` dial-delay knob set longer than the rendered ping interval plus timeout, which the T5 and T8 cells need. The Tier 8 "warming pod" SIGSTOP case became the P3 T8 cell on the service-mode probe pool — BECAUSE the old case named no phase, and a SIGSTOP after `ready` on a pod with no warm-up does not keep it before READY — ALTERNATIVES: a supervisor-side delay knob (the supervisor is not the probe and has no test hooks).
WATCHOUT: do not restore pre-READY order, exit, or end wording in CODE-3 bullets, CODE-4, or TEST-1; cite a table cell. Do not move `net.Listen` before the gates. The reason for the `tcpSocket` probe on every sidecar pod and for the handler-before-gates order is one sentence in summary D-READY; a `grpc` probe fails on an mTLS adapter, and a startup or liveness probe fails the pod under `restartPolicy: Never` (the alternatives the CODE-4 bullet used to list).
DECISION (redesign-remedy review, 2026-10-23): the OPEN window between the `ready` claim and the bind is closed by moving the claim after `net.Listen` and making it under the lock that guards `notLive`, with `notLive` read in the same critical section — BECAUSE the bind is READY (CODE-4), so a cause in the old claim-to-bind window was before READY and §4.7.10 **Liveness.** requires the adapter to exit non-zero there, while the old table took the **Start** cell; a cause between the bind and the claim now exits the adapter, which is fail-closed, and on a pod the probe has already marked Ready the WarmPoolController's **Exit** preempts **Start** as §4.7.10 **Liveness.** states — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it as a narrow fail-closed deviation (a cell that contradicts the converged table).
DECISION (redesign-remedy review, 2026-10-23): the exit claim also covers a `net.Listen` error, a `srv.Serve` error returned with the phase `ready`, and a post-READY service-mode cause (each moves the word to `failed` before exiting 1), and the handler cancels the gate context only after its claim — BECAUSE a cancel before the claim lets a gate return first and `main` win `failed`, exiting 1 with no `end` on a SIGTERM cell, and an unclaimed listen, serve, or service-mode exit is a second exit path that can cut a running `ExitOnSignal` short — ALTERNATIVES: none considered.
WATCHOUT: the window between the bind and the kubelet's first passing probe remains: a cause there is after READY by the adapter's definition while the WarmPoolController still sees `warming` or `sdk_connecting`. That residue rests on settled **Warm-fill causes precede READY** and the precursor fix, not on this table.

### [redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.2]
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): a new **CRD artifacts** subsection under the staged schema changes holds one table (Runtime, SandboxTemplate, SandboxClaim rows; columns: authoring Go type file and edit, chart manifest from `make generate`, embedded re-copy, drift tests), with one paragraph stating the hand re-application of the `lenny.dev/schema-version` annotation and top-level preserve-unknown markers and that `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml` carries no CRD. CODE-1 and CODE-5 **Targets.** point at its rows; CODE-1's "the CRD manifests are regenerated" clause is deleted. The SandboxTemplate row names `sandboxtemplate_types.go` `RecyclePolicy.RuntimeProcess` with `+kubebuilder:validation:Enum=keep;restart` — BECAUSE three prose attributions drifted (`[non-spec.3.fix-G3.1]`) and none named the SandboxTemplate type file (`[non-spec.3.review-applicability.1]`) — ALTERNATIVES: rewording each Targets paragraph (the drift that produced the findings).
FACT: controller-gen does not emit the `lenny.dev/schema-version` annotation or the top-level `spec`/`status` preserve-unknown markers; `TestCRDManifestsInSyncWithGoTypes` strips them before comparing, and its diagnosis tells the implementor to re-apply them after `make generate` — EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/crds_test.go postProcessLine, TestCRDManifestsInSyncWithGoTypes; commit 56a1a10c7 message.
WATCHOUT: CODE-1 still lists `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml`, because its chart catalog edit changes the dev-preset render; that is the chart-edit case the table paragraph names, not a CRD change.

### [redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.3]
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): the webhook refuses a read-write mount of the supervisor volume on every container except an `initContainers` entry named `lenny-supervisor-stage` whose `restartPolicy` is not `Always`. `podsecurity.ContainerSpec` gains `Init` and `RestartPolicyAlways`, which `translatePodSpec` sets for each init container. Tier 1 and Tier 9 name the refused restartable container as `lenny-supervisor-stage` — BECAUSE a restartable init container runs for the pod's life, so admitting it by name keeps a writer on the volume the runtime container executes the supervisor from; the staged §13.1 text describes "the init container" that copies the binary, which a container that never completes is not; fail closed. Closes the WATCHOUT in `[non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]` — ALTERNATIVES: admit by name (the gap that WATCHOUT names); one combined field such as `RunsToCompletion` (hides which pod-spec field the rule reads).

### [redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.4]

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `AdmitTenantPin` reports whether a refused pod must drain (lifetime helper refusal, or pool reuses no pods), and `claimIdle` calls `DrainRefusedPod` on that report in place of `!req.KeepsRuntime` — BECAUSE after the rename a sequential `restart` pool has `ReusesPods` true, so a used `keep` pod refused by the lifetime helper was never stamped, contradicting staged §5.2 "every acquisition refuses such a pod and stamps its drain request" — ALTERNATIVES: gate on `!RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` (would drain a `restart` pod pinned to another tenant); re-run the helper in claimIdle (second Pod Get, two deciders); stamp inside AdmitTenantPin (it writes nothing and runs inside the fallback's row lock).
FACT: claimIdle's only drain gate is `if !req.KeepsRuntime { DrainRefusedPod(...) }` after a refused AdmitTenantPin; AdmitTenantPin returns a bare (bool, error); its callers are claimIdle, SlotClaimer (slotclaimer.go ClaimRequest literal), and the fallback admit closure (fallbackclaim.go `return podclaim.AdmitTenantPin(...)`) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:189-197; tenantpin.go:50; slotclaimer.go:536; podsession/fallbackclaim.go:168
WATCHOUT: a used `keep` pod is lifetime-refused whatever tenant pins it and must be stamped; only a `restart` (or unaffected) pod refused for its tenant or `unassigned` goes unstamped. The finding's suggested text "never on a pod pinned to another tenant" is wrong for a `keep` pod — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md §5.2 Deployer acknowledgment staged paragraph ("every acquisition refuses such a pod and stamps its drain request")
WATCHOUT: the `DrainRefusedPod` doc comment (tenantpin.go) and the claimIdle comment above the call (claimer.go "On a pool outside the process-reuse rule") both state the old pool-keyed drain reason; CODE-5's comment-rewrite bullet names neither explicitly (its lead clause covers the "keeps no runtime process across sessions" phrase the DrainRefusedPod comment uses). Design marked that bullet not-a-site; a later round may add both to its list — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/tenantpin.go DrainRefusedPod comment; claimer.go:184-188

Not edited this round because the spec staging is frozen.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: claimIdle drains a refused pod only under `if !req.KeepsRuntime`; AdmitTenantPin returns a bare bool, so after the ReusesPods rename a lifetime-refused used `keep` pod on a sequential `restart` pool is never stamped. DECISION: AdmitTenantPin reports whether a refused pod must drain (the pool reuses no pods, or the lifetime refusal), and claimIdle stamps DrainRefusedPod on that report alone, dropping its pool-keyed gate — BECAUSE one decision point carries both refusal kinds; the slot pass and the fallback ignore the report — ALTERNATIVES: gating claimIdle on `!RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` (would drain a `restart` pod pinned to another tenant or `unassigned`); a second label read in claimIdle (double Get, races the first read); stamping inside AdmitTenantPin (it writes nothing and runs inside the fallback row lock).

The reviewer's "never on a pod pinned to another tenant" holds only for a `restart` pod; do not apply it to a `keep` pod. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:789

DOC-1 still does not name it; this is the Deferred entry `[spec.8.follow-up-fix.1]`, which was never applied.

DOC-1 documents `recycle.runtimeProcess: restart` in execution-modes.md, and troubleshooting item 2 tells operators that only `keep` needs the acknowledgment, while the shipped admission refuses `restart` with 400 until 0084 lands. ### [non-spec.4.review-fresh.1]

FACT: The tree's preStop hook (pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go) drains by sending SIGTERM to PID 1 of the hooked container and polling until it exits, so "the arrival of the preStop hook or the stop signal" (the `deleting` set point) is one SIGTERM path into the adapter's handler; the runtime container has no preStop and gets the kubelet stop signal at once, which the supervisor ignores (Signals kubelet row). — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go:1-60; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:95
FACT: `createPod` fails outright when the Runtime CRD Get fails (no fallback), while `resolveTemplate` is best-effort; so CODE-4's `RuntimeCommand`/`RuntimeEnv`/`ExecutionMode` always come from a live CRD, and only `RuntimeProcess` (template-mirrored) can fall back to `keep`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:348-352, :289-305
FACT: The Runtime CRD `executionMode` is optional with no `+kubebuilder:default`; "Empty defaults to `session` at registration time". The shared `restartable` helper (CODE-1/CODE-4) must decide how it reads an empty value; both sides use one helper, so admission and pod creation cannot disagree. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go:34-41
FACT: No tier-0 or tier-11 test pins spec text that SPEC-2 through SPEC-14 delete, other than `claimOwnershipRowSentence` (TEST-2) and the lifecycle.md pin in runtime_session_end_paths_doc_test.go (TEST-1 tier 11). The claim-register gate validates row form only, so landing the CH-SUPERVISE card at S9 before CODE-3's WIRED rows at S16 fails no gate. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/claim_register_test.go:240-300; grep of tests/tier11_docs and tests/tier0_static for each replaced find string
FACT: Every webhook that inspects images or UIDs flattens init containers; the egress-capture test sidecar injects no env into the runtime container, so dropping the runtime container's `Env` under supervision loses nothing it adds. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/egress_capture.go:76-120
DECISION: Filed one (d): docs/operator-guide/namespace-and-isolation.md:96 enumerates the lenny-label-immutability labels verbatim from §17.2 item 5 and is in no DOC-1 bullet; SPEC-5 adds `lenny.dev/runtime-process` to item 5. — BECAUSE the DOC-1 grep list reaches that page only through its line-261 hit — ALTERNATIVES: the chart template comment at label-immutability-webhook.yaml:9 (a comment, and already missing dns-policy; not filed).
WATCHOUT: CODE-3's keep-pod clause in `ServesNextSession()` (false when the composed SocketRuntimeProcess's is) can report `runtime_live: false` with `notLive` unset after a successful `DemoteSDK` end and no relaunch; every path I found to a scrub in that state is unreachable (a failed or unpublished session releases without reuse), so not filed. A later lens that finds a reachable path should file it against the **Liveness.** predicate. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-459, :493-512; non-spec-changes.md:69
USEFUL [non-spec.3.review-kubernetes.1]: ExitOnSignal's nil-GracefulStopper tolerance and the absence of SDKWarmReady consumers saved re-deriving the pre-READY handler safety.

### [non-spec.4.review-kubernetes.1]

- FACT: `resolveTemplate` is best-effort and returns nil on any miss, and `createPod` then falls back to builder defaults. CODE-4's `RuntimeProcess` read from the template therefore falls back to `keep` (the label value that refuses) on a template miss. The pod is refused at acquisition and drained, so the fallback causes churn and exposes nothing. It was judged not a finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:283-306, :363
- FACT: the chart CRDs carry top-level `spec`/`status` `x-kubernetes-preserve-unknown-fields: true`. The tier-2 "returns it unpruned" case for `runtimeProcess` therefore passes even without the Go type change. The discriminating check is the tier-3 `crd_schema` case. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/crds/lenny.dev_runtimes.yaml:64, charts/lenny/crds/lenny.dev_sandboxtemplates.yaml:62
- FACT: `lenny-pod-security` matches Pod CREATE/UPDATE and pods/ephemeralcontainers UPDATE in agent namespaces, and `translatePodSpec` flattens init, regular, and ephemeral containers. CODE-4's `Init` flag cleanly separates the stage init container from an ephemeral container that reuses its name. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/admission-policies/pod-security-webhook.yaml:40-70, pkg/admission/webhook/pod_security.go:93-131
- FACT: Decide leaves `idle` on PodNotReady, and `syncReadinessGate` only ever sets the gate True. A transient readiness-probe failure from CODE-4's new adapter `tcpSocket` probe therefore moves no Sandbox phase. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:88-97, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:728-750
- DECISION: returned no findings under the Kubernetes-idiom lens. BECAUSE the field-ownership writes (PSC writes SandboxTemplate.spec, the WPC writes the pod label at create, the gateway deletes SandboxClaim on a refused rebind) match the §4.6.3 table, no status field is used as a command, no finalizer is added, and no synchronous path waits on a reconcile. ALTERNATIVES: a finding on the readiness-probe window between `net.Listen` and the P5 claim (microsecond window; retires through Exit). Rejected as speculative.

### [non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `Claimer.claimIdle` stamps the drain request only under `if !req.KeepsRuntime` after `AdmitTenantPin` refuses a pod, and `AdmitTenantPin` returns a bare bool, so the caller cannot tell a lifetime refusal from a tenant-mismatch refusal. CODE-5 renames the field `ReusesPods`, which is true on a sequential `restart` pool, so a used `keep` pod refused there is never drained, although CODE-5 says "the existing `DrainRefusedPod` path drains it" and staged SPEC-5(g) requires the stamp. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:186-197; tenantpin.go:51-71; non-spec-changes.md:178-179; spec-changes.md:789
WATCHOUT: the drain must be stamped only for the lifetime refusal. A same-pool pod pinned to another tenant stays pinned for that tenant, and draining it would break tenant reuse. The fix needs a refusal reason from `AdmitTenantPin` (or a separate helper call in `claimIdle`), not a widened `!ReusesPods` gate.
FACT: slotclaimer.go:536-538 builds its `ClaimRequest` literal with `KeepsRuntime: true`. After the split, a zero `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` would refuse every used concurrent pod. Existing slotclaimer tests pin pods to tenants (slotclaimer_test.go:108), so they would likely catch it. Not filed.
FACT: the tree's `ShutdownDemoteSDK` calls the server RPC `s.DemoteSDK` (sdkwarm.go:88-92), so a SIGTERM end on a `preConnect` pod passes through `DemoteSDK` and sets `endDemote` under CODE-3. It does not go straight to `EndProcess`. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:69-104
WATCHOUT (not filed, spec-side residue): READY is the bind, but the WarmPoolController sees `warming` until the kubelet's first passing probe. In that window, a link failure on a session-mode `keep` pod without a container termination (a SIGSTOPped supervisor, or a protocol failure) does not exit the adapter, and the pod retires through **Start** rather than the `warming` cell's **Exit**. This is a narrow window and is recorded in `[redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.1]`.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, Standing Settled **Rebind label read**]: these established that the rebind stamps no drain and leaves the stamp to the next idle scan, which is the scan whose gate is wrong.

- FACT: `AdmitTenantPin` today refuses every pinned pod when `KeepsRuntime` is false (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/tenantpin.go:62), which is the one-session-only control on non-reusing pools; CODE-5's rename to `ReusesPods` preserves it. `SlotClaimer` passes a literal `KeepsRuntime: true` (slotclaimer.go:537); the implementor must also set `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment: true` there or the new label helper refuses every pinned `keep` pod on concurrent pools (fail-closed, availability only, not filed). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:537

TEST-1 pins only the permissive case (tier 3, no `--adapter-uid`). — BECAUSE: both checks are fail-closed security predicates the spec card states, and the D-AUTH argument against a forged `ended` depends on them. — ALTERNATIVES: filing a name-only exemption gap in the pod-security mount-rule tests (no regular or ephemeral container named `lenny-supervisor-stage`). Rejected as speculative, because the supervisor binary is exec'd once at container start and `restartPolicy: Never` means a later writer gains nothing.

### [non-spec.6.fix-G3.1]
FACT: The chart controller-argv gate parse-checks a conditional controller flag only when a `chartFlagRenders` value set turns its branch on; `maximal` sets `controller.adapterImage` and `controller.egressCaptureImage` today, so `controller.supervisorImage` must be added there — EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/controller_chart_flags_test.go chartFlagRenders; dev_profile_render_test.go TestDevProfileTagBasedImagesAndAdapter pins only `--adapter-image`.
DECISION: The worker-descriptor test is a separate clause rather than a widening of the "0, 1, and 2" clause — BECAUSE the sweep worker legitimately holds `openat` directory descriptors, and it never calls execve, so SOCK_CLOEXEC does not protect it and the post-exec child test cannot observe it — ALTERNATIVES: tier-9 probe (agent UID cannot read the supervisor UID's /proc fd entries).
DECISION: The `cleanup` request bound is pinned by extending the scrub-table `cleanup_timeout` case with a reply delivered after `timeoutMs` (real delay, short `cleanupTimeoutSeconds`) — BECAUSE that is the one input that discriminates a bound at or below the deadline — ALTERNATIVES: injected clock in the fake peer (new mechanism); asserting ctx Deadline() (forces an implementation).
OPEN: CODE-3 scrub bullet IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE requires the scrub sequence to fit inside the gateway's missing-report timeout (`cleanupTimeoutSeconds` plus its grace), but the grace is gateway-side and operator-tunable (`RecycleBoundaryCoordinator.GracePeriod`, default `MissingReportGracePeriod`, pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go) and never reaches the adapter, so the adapter can neither satisfy nor test it. A later round should name a fixed margin or drop the clause (an overrun fails closed: the gateway retires the pod).

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: `ShutdownDemoteSDK` acts only on an empty registry, by calling `demoteIdleSDK` directly; with a session registered it returns without demoting. One sentence goes in the CODE-3 SDK-warm bullet, P5's "`ExitOnSignal` as today" becomes a pointer to that bullet, and one Tier 1 fake-peer case is added — BECAUSE §6.1 **Adapter SIGTERM behavior during `sdk_connecting`.** scopes the SIGTERM demotion to a pod that holds no session, and the kubelet row of §4.7.10 **Signals.** and D-PODEXIT need a held session's runtime and slot tree to survive until the adapter exits, so the eviction checkpoint has something to read. This is a behavior change in the one shared function. It does not narrow any existing sentence — ALTERNATIVES: setting disposition `deleting` before the demote (the session is still ended and its tree removed, so the checkpoint is still lost); clearing `sdkConnected` when a session binds (that flag also gates the rule-8 refuse arm and the `DemoteSDK` RPC semantics, so changing it widens the blast radius); a supervised-only branch (the embedded preconnect-echo binary shares the defect and the same function, and the project ships one implementation per concern).
FACT: today `ShutdownDemoteSDK` calls the `DemoteSDK` RPC handler. With an entry registered, that handler writes `session_end`, tears down, and runs `releaseSessionSlotUnderGuard` (deregistration and slot tree removal). preStop sends SIGTERM to the adapter as its first act, so this happens at the very start of the drain. The defect exists in the tree today for embedded preConnect runtimes (`cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo`) as well — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go ShutdownDemoteSDK, DemoteSDK, demoteIdleSDK; pkg/adapter/slotsession.go finishSlotRelease; pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go runPreStop; pkg/adapter/exit.go ExitOnSignal
WATCHOUT: implement the empty-registry branch as a direct `demoteIdleSDK` call. Do not implement it as a guard followed by `s.DemoteSDK`, because the handler re-reads the registry and a session that registers between the two reads gets the full demotion back. The existing TestShutdownDemoteSDKDemotesPreConnected registers no session and stays green — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/shutdown_demote_test.go
OPEN: §28.5.3 **Session frame writes.**, the `DemoteSDK` guard-timeout row (spec/28_communication-channels.md, "the SIGTERM path force-terminates the SDK process"), together with the `DemoteSDK` doc comment in pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go, names the SIGTERM path as a caller that can reach the slot guard. After this fix it never takes the guard, so that clause describes an unreachable case. The clause is vacuous rather than contradictory. The spec loop is frozen, so a later proposal should drop the clause. The code comment is edited when CODE-3 lands.

FACT: The CODE-4 readiness chain holds in the tree: `syncReadinessGate` flips the one-way `lenny.dev/sandbox-ready` gate only after `ContainersReady`, `Decide` moves `warming` to `idle` and `DecideSDKWarm` moves warm-fill `sdk_connecting` to `idle` only on `PodReady`, and `idle` with `PodNotReady` takes no action. ### [non-spec.6.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: The pool-reuse predicate reaches ClaimRequest through a chain of fields, not one site: `sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go` computes `mirror.KeepsRuntime`, `podsession/resolve.go` PoolMatch carries it, `sessionserver/bind_request.go` and `resume_rebind.go` copy it into BindRequest/ResumeRequest, and `podsession/bindclaim.go`/`resume.go` copy it into `podclaim.ClaimRequest`; `podclaim/slotclaimer.go` builds its own literal with `KeepsRuntime: true`. Any second predicate (CODE-5's `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment`) must be threaded through every hop, or its zero value refuses every used `keep` pod. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:79, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_request.go:150, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:536-537, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindclaim.go:65
WATCHOUT: CODE-5 says only "the claim request carries RequiresProcessAcknowledgment" and renames the `KeepsRuntime` fields to `ReusesPods`; it names no source for the new flag on the mirror, PoolMatch, BindRequest, ResumeRequest, or the slot-pass literal. Filed this round. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:178-179
FACT: `ShutdownDemoteSDK` calls the Server RPC handler `s.DemoteSDK`, which calls `sw.DemoteSDK`; on a supervised pod every DemoteSDK path therefore reaches `SupervisedRuntime.DemoteSDK`, so CODE-3's "call the same SupervisedRuntime.DemoteSDK" holds (indirectly for ShutdownDemoteSDK). — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:70-92, :376-400
FACT: The preStop hook only sends SIGTERM to PID 1 of the adapter container, so the `deleting` disposition's "preStop hook or stop signal" is one code site: the SIGTERM handler. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go (preStopProcessID = 1, signal func)

DECISION: filed the Open item "ShutdownDemoteSDK on a claimed preConnect pod" as a finding — BECAUSE the tree confirms it: `sdkConnected` is cleared only at sdkwarm.go:105, :315, :399, :416 (never by ConfigureWorkspace or a session start), `ExitOnSignal` calls `ShutdownDemoteSDK` first (exit.go:29-36), and `Server.DemoteSDK` with a registered session writes `session_end`, calls `sw.DemoteSDK`, and runs `releaseSessionSlotUnderGuard`, which removes the slot tree (sdkwarm.go:357-402, slotsession.go:350-378). FACT: the agent preStop subcommand only SIGTERMs PID 1 and polls (pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go); pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop is the GATEWAY pod's preStop, not the agent pod's. ### [non-spec.6.review-security.1]
- FACT: `translatePodSpec` already translates initContainers and ephemeralContainers into `podsecurity.PodSpec.Containers`, and the reserved-UID rule refuses only the adapter and agent UIDs, so the CODE-4 stage UID and the CODE-6 preflight pod pass the identity clause as long as runAsUser/runAsGroup are non-zero. EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/webhook/pod_security.go:112-127; pkg/podsecurity/podsecurity.go:412-425
- FACT: cosign-verify covers init containers, so the supervisor stage image falls under image verification with no webhook change. EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/webhook/cosign_verify.go:60-61
- FACT: ClaimRequest zero values fail closed after the CODE-5 split: `ReusesPods` false refuses every pinned pod, and `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` false refuses a pinned pod that is not labelled `restart`. A caller that leaves a field unset therefore refuses too much and never admits too much. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/tenantpin.go:59-71; slotclaimer.go:536-537 (the slot pass builds a literal with `KeepsRuntime: true`)
- UNVERIFIED: the slot idle pass literal (slotclaimer.go:536) must also set `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment: true` after the split, or a concurrent `keep` pool refuses its own used pods. This is an availability problem, not a security one, so it was not filed under this lens. A correctness reviewer should check whether CODE-5 states the literal.
- DECISION: no security findings in round 6. BECAUSE the round's edits (cloud build/render scripts, the supervisor exiting without `ready` on an SO_PEERCRED mismatch, the AdmitTenantPin drain report, the probe ENV, and the tier-2 CEL cases) keep the same controls or tighten them. ALTERNATIVES: filing the missing ephemeral-container case for the supervisor-volume mount rule was rejected, because `translatePodSpec` runs the ephemeral container through the same per-container rule, and the existing tests already cover a regular container.

### [prune-remedy.2026-10-23.1]
DECISION: pruned non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 **Tier 0.** (388 to 130 characters) and **Tier 1.** (12,843 characters on one line to 7,948 characters in grouped sub-bullets). Hand-enumerated case lists that mirrored a home became per-home coverage rules: one case per cell of the CODE-3 **Pre-READY sequence** table, the §5.2 **Supervised scrub sequence.** table, and the §4.7.10 **Liveness.** and **Children.** tables; one per row of **Runtime exits.** and **Signals.**; one per decode failure of §28.5.3 **Degradation.**; one per branch of each CODE-1, CODE-4, and CODE-5 rule; and one per stated behavior or clause of the CODE-2 and CODE-3 bullets. Each group names only the inputs that no home derives (for example the held-session `ShutdownDemoteSDK` case, the `cleanup_timeout` fake-clock case, the tenant-stamping cases, and the kill-reach grandchild that already exited). The gating-property set is spelled "a gating property" with its home at §4.7.11 item 8 **Property self-test.** in CODE-2, CODE-3, CODE-6, and TEST-1. Summary D-SCOPE now states that the shared `ShutdownDemoteSDK` change reaches embedded `preConnect` pods, and D-PODEXIT points at the kubelet row instead of stating a narrower mechanism — BECAUSE the introspection prune verdict found that nine of eighteen non-spec findings came from the enumerated Tier 1 bullet and that the class stopped recurring wherever a coverage rule replaced an enumeration — ALTERNATIVES: further facet fixes to the enumeration (rejected by the verdict).
DECISION: later fixers must not re-enumerate a case that a coverage rule covers. A missing case is fixed by naming its home in the rule or, when no home derives its discriminating input, by adding it to the group's named inputs.

### [prune-remedy.2026-10-23.2]
DECISION: a coverage audit of the prune restored, as named inputs or by naming their home, the cases no home derived — BECAUSE the old text asserted them and the cited homes do not state the discriminating input or outcome: in **Tier 0.**, the matrix, identifier, naming-lint, and claim-register gates (no CODE target names them), the controller-argv `maximal` set, and `TestDevProfileTagBasedImagesAndAdapter`; in admission, the sequential and concurrent pools, the admitted `keep` pool, and the seed's `SEED_VALIDATION` code; in `SupervisedRuntime`, `ConfigureWorkspace` launching once, the `refuseUnconfirmedSDKWarmStart` single `end` with no `runtime_crash`, and `ShutdownDemoteSDK` on an embedded `preConnect` pod (summary D-SCOPE); the **Crash.** window assertions; the three non-restartable `createPod` inputs, the `type: mcp` and embedded no-render pods, and `Build`'s empty-image refusal; and in the C harness, the signal-handling, reaping, `cleanup`, and self-test bullets, which the pruned list omitted, with "launch-environment bullet" retargeted to the Modes bullet, which holds it. The DemoteSDK guard-timeout and `end`-error cases, the rebind error, the second end and sweep after a `cleaned` error, a `launch` refused while `notLive` is set, and each **Liveness.** channel's outcome are stated by their homes (the CODE-3 SDK-warm bullet, the §5.2 table, the **Launch.** permit, and the **Liveness.** channel list) and stay rule-covered — ALTERNATIVES: none.

The adapter receives only cleanupTimeoutSeconds (pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:174). The CODE-3 scrub-deadline IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE therefore cannot be checked by the adapter, and the cleanup bound alone already exceeds cleanupTimeoutSeconds. The §5.2 **Supervised scrub sequence.** intro already states that a sequence that outlasts the timeout retires the pod.
FACT: No OpenAPI request validation runs on the admin pool API (no kin-openapi or validator under pkg/gateway). validateRecyclePolicy rejects unknown scrubProfile and onScrubFailure values through IsValid() (pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/poolstore.go:628-637), but CODE-5 states no such check for runtimeProcess. It reports false for a demoted or never-connected keep pod. Do not file it: every reachable case follows a start that failed before acceptance, and that release ends without reuse anyway (§4.6.3 binding clause).

These comments state "the runtime process lives as long as the pod" or "is not re-created" citing §4.7.10, and SPEC-2(a) makes them false for a `restart` pod: pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:26, socketruntime.go:28,64,286,461,469, session.go:274,450,549, embedded.go:237 (embedded, stays true), cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:342, pkg/sandbox/podscrub/podscrub.go:453 ("nothing inside the pod starts it again"), tests/tier7a_load_local/{coordinator_hold_termination_race_test.go:177,sole_session_concurrent_release_test.go:113,socket_runtime_reader_stop_race_test.go:8}, tests/tier5_e2e_kind/execution_modes_test.go:207, tests/tier10_conformance/recycle_scrub_conformance_test.go:9. ### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed agenda item (1), an unknown `recycle.runtimeProcess` value — BECAUSE the tree's `validateRecyclePolicy` checks every other recycle enum with `IsValid()` (poolstore.go:628, :635), the admin handlers do no OpenAPI request validation, and CODE-5 adds no domain check. A value such as `Keep` is therefore stored, `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` reads it as not `keep`, and the PSC's `syncTemplate` `CreateOrUpdate` (poolscaling/controller.go:609) is refused by the staged CRD `Enum=keep;restart` on every reconcile. The pool wedges, and PoolConfigDrift is its only signal — ALTERNATIVES: filing it under security as an acknowledgment bypass (left to that lens; the remedy is the same one-line check).
DECISION: did not file agenda item (2), the scrub-deadline IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE bounded by a gateway grace the adapter cannot know — BECAUSE an overrun fails closed: the gateway's `expireMissingReport` retires the pod (recycleboundary.go:367), and an unanswered request becomes a link failure that sets `notLive`. Nothing is lost, leaked, or stalled. The clause is unverifiable rather than unsafe, so dropping it or naming a fixed margin is the single-source or test lens's call.
FACT: `resolveTemplate` returns nil on any template lookup miss, and `createPod` then falls back to the builder defaults (controller.go:372-378, :452). A transient miss creates a `keep`-labelled pod for a `restart` pool. That is fail-closed: the per-pod refusal drains the pod after its first use. The Runtime CRD read, by contrast, fails `createPod` closed (controller.go:439) — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:439,452
FACT: `sw.DemoteSDK` and `endRuntimeForHoldTimeout`'s `CloseListener` run outside `s.mu` (sdkwarm.go:311,389,412; holdstate.go:201,241). So the CODE-3 end handlers, which take `s.mu`, cannot deadlock against an `end` caller waiting for its `ended` reply — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:389

### [non-spec.7.review-test-coverage.1]
DECISION: filed agenda item (1) as two missing-test findings: an unknown `recycle.runtimeProcess` value at pool admission (CODE-5 states no rejection, and `RequiresProcessAcknowledgment` "recycling under keep" lets an unknown value skip the acknowledgment), and an unknown `lenny.dev/runtime-process` label value at acquisition (staged §5.2 "reads an absent or unknown value of the label as `keep`"; TEST-1 lists only keep, restart, absent) — BECAUSE both are fail-closed boundaries a plausible implementation gets wrong while passing every listed case — ALTERNATIVES: filing agenda (2) the scrub-deadline constraint as untestable (left to the mechanism lens); (3) and (4) are edit-site items.

### [redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.5]
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23): the pod-builder-to-adapter input contract has one home, the CODE-3 **Adapter inputs** table (rows `--supervise-socket`, `--runtime-process`, `--launch-mode`, `--supervise-ping-interval`, `--supervise-ping-timeout`, `--warm-up-timeout`, and the existing `--require-so-peercred` and `--runtime-uid` that 0087 keys new behavior on; columns domain, rendered on and from which `podspec.Inputs` field, absent, outside the domain, and every keyed adapter behavior). D-SDKWARM, D-SERVICE, D-PODLIFETIME, D-SURFACE, the CODE-3 `SupervisedRuntime`, scrub, SDK-warm, and main.go bullets, the deleted CODE-3 Service mode bullet, the Pre-READY P1 and P2 cells and End-before-READY bullet, and CODE-4's `Inputs` and `applySupervisor` bullets point at it. TEST-1 Tier 1 gains one coverage rule over the table (each domain value, absent, and one outside value) that leaves rendering to the Pod builder cases and phase order to the `RunPreReady` cases — BECAUSE the contract was patched a facet per round (breaker in round 3, service mode in round 7, the breaker-open `preConnect` and unknown `--runtime-process` facets left as UNVERIFIED and WATCHOUT in round 7), and D-SDKWARM and D-SERVICE had drifted from CODE-3 (stop verdict `0087-r7-stop.json`) — ALTERNATIVES: patching the two open facets in place (the one-facet-per-round pattern the verdict names).
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23), facet (a): `--warm-up-launch` (`preconnect` or `service`, absent otherwise) is replaced by `--launch-mode`, rendered on every supervised pod with one of `session`, `service`, `preconnect-sdk-warm`, and `preconnect-pod-warm`, and `Inputs.ExecutionMode` and `Inputs.WarmUpLaunch` by `Inputs.LaunchMode`. `createPod` sets `preconnect-sdk-warm` when the Sandbox's `resolveSDKWarm` result is `sdkWarmActive()` and `preconnect-pod-warm` otherwise, so a `preConnect` pod created while the breaker is open makes no warm-up launch (§6.1 **Circuit-breaker for SDK-warm pools.**), still carries `SDKWarmRuntime`, and sets `sdkConnected` at its step-7 `ConfigureWorkspace` launch. `SDKWarmRuntime` moves to a wrapper `main` uses on the two `preconnect-*` values only, so `session` and `service` pods answer `ConfigureWorkspace` and `DemoteSDK` with `Unimplemented`. An absent or unknown value is a P1 failure. TEST-1 Tier 5's breaker-open case passes: the pod skips P3 and P4, and the gateway's `ConfigureWorkspace` (keyed on the runtime's capability) launches at step 7 — BECAUSE an absent flag could not tell a breaker-open `preConnect` pod from a session-mode pod, which left `sdkConnected` and the `ExitOnSignal` branch undecidable; an explicit value on every supervised pod means absence never carries meaning; fail closed — ALTERNATIVES: a second boolean flag beside `--warm-up-launch` (invalid combinations such as `service` with no warm-up); keeping the name `--warm-up-launch` with a no-launch value (names a launch on pods that make none).
DECISION (redesign-remedy, 2026-10-23), facet (b): `--runtime-process` loses its `keep` default. An absent value, a value other than `keep` or `restart`, and `restart` beside a `--launch-mode` other than `session` (a pod `createPod` never builds, because such a runtime is not `restartable`) are each a P1 failure — BECAUSE a default or a silent coercion would let a mis-rendered pod run under a lifetime nobody chose, and the label the gateway reads already carries the value explicitly; fail closed — ALTERNATIVES: reading unknown as `keep` as the acquisition reads the label (the adapter would then skip the `restart` property gate and the boundary end on a pod the gateway admits as `restart`).
WATCHOUT: the ping flags are rendered from `podspec` constants. `.claude/rules/code-best-practices.md` asks for an operator override of a non-spec default; the adapter flag is overridable only by changing the controller. A later round may add controller flags if the reviewers read the rule that way.

### [redesign-remedy-verify.2026-10-23.6]
FACT: the CODE-3 **Adapter inputs** table has a row for every adapter flag CODE-4 renders (`--supervise-socket`, `--runtime-process`, `--launch-mode`, `--supervise-ping-interval`, `--supervise-ping-timeout`, and the existing `--require-so-peercred` and `--runtime-uid`); no `--warm-up-launch`, `WarmUpLaunch`, or `Inputs.ExecutionMode` remains outside historical ledger entries — EVIDENCE: grep of the proposal directory.
FACT: the gateway keys `ConfigureWorkspace` on the runtime's `preConnect` and not on the pod (`req.PreConnect && !req.Demoted`), so a `preconnect-pod-warm` pod serves its first session through a step-7 launch and the Tier 5 breaker-open case can pass — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go:88-92.
FACT: `resolveSDKWarm` reads any Runtime lookup error as not `preConnect` and any pool lookup error as breaker closed — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go:70-98.
DECISION: the transient-lookup outcome (`preconnect-pod-warm`) is stated once, in the `--launch-mode` row — BECAUSE it serves sessions at the cost of a step-7 launch and never strands a pod in `sdk_connecting`, which is the fail-safe direction — ALTERNATIVES: failing `createPod` on a lookup error (delays fill for a latency-only gain).
DECISION: the ping interval and deadline become controller flags (`--supervise-ping-interval`, `--supervise-ping-timeout`, `controller.tuning` chart keys, default the `podspec` constants) carried in `Inputs` — BECAUSE §28.5.3 **Timing.** states no value for either and `.claude/rules/code-best-practices.md` requires an operator override of a non-spec default — ALTERNATIVES: leaving them unrendered with `no-spec-value` rows, as `--heartbeat-interval-seconds` is (the rule gives no exception for that precedent). This resolves the ping WATCHOUT of `[redesign-remedy.2026-10-23.5]`.
DECISION: `agentSocketPeerAuth` counting the supervise socket is consistent with the spec: the §28.5.3 `CH-SUPERVISE` **Endpoint.** admits only the agent UID while `SO_PEERCRED` is enforced, and §13.1 **Container identity.** forbids UID 0; TEST-1's Adapter inputs rule gains the discriminating named input (supervise socket as the only agent socket, zero UID, both `--require-so-peercred` values).
DECISION: the `--runtime-process` row's keyed-behavior cell names the three reading sites instead of restating their `keep` and `restart` branches, which the `AwaitSupervisor` bullet, the scrub bullet, and **Adapter state for one pod** state — BECAUSE each rule is stated once.
DECISION: the `--launch-mode` rename is sound: the four values partition every supervised pod, no spec text or tree symbol binds "launch mode", and an explicit value on every pod makes absence a failure.
WATCHOUT: `--warm-up-timeout` is also a non-spec default that no pod renders (`no-spec-value` row); the same rule reads on it, and FIXTURE-1's dial delay is sized against its compiled default.

### [non-spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the `--launch-mode` row derives the value from the Runtime CRD `createPod` already reads (fail-closed Get at the top of `createPod`) and the pool's `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` read at creation. A failed pool read on a `preConnect` runtime creates no pod. BECAUSE SPEC-2 **SDK-warm.** fixes the warm-up launch from the breaker state at creation, and with that state unknown neither `preconnect-*` value conforms; the WarmPoolController retries. ALTERNATIVES: render `preconnect-pod-warm` on a failed read (departs from SPEC-2 when the breaker is closed); make `resolveSDKWarm` fail closed (widens into the WPC `sdk_connecting` route, whose best-effort contract D-SDKWARM relies on).
CORRECTS [non-spec.7 DECISION "the transient-lookup outcome (`preconnect-pod-warm`) is stated once, in the `--launch-mode` row"]: that outcome is withdrawn. `createPod` does not call `resolveSDKWarm` for the launch mode. `resolveSDKWarm` reads a Runtime-read error as not-preConnect and a pool-read error as breaker-closed, so it broke SPEC-2 in both directions. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go `resolveSDKWarm`; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `createPod`.
WATCHOUT: do not restore a lookup-error or latency-defence sentence in the `--launch-mode` row, and do not make `createPod`'s other pool reads (`resolveTemplate`, the resource class) fail closed. Only the `sdkWarmDisabled` read on a `preConnect` runtime refuses. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 **Adapter inputs** `--launch-mode` row.
DECISION: the P5 signal cell routes `ExitOnSignal` through the `--launch-mode` row and no longer states its own kubelet ending. BECAUSE that row is the one home of the per-pod branch (`ShutdownDemoteSDK` on `preconnect-*`, `EndProcess` on `service`, the kubelet ending on `session`). The old cell named `ShutdownDemoteSDK` and the kubelet ending for every pod. ALTERNATIVES: keep the kubelet clause, or scope it to `session` (both are second statements of the row's session branch).
WATCHOUT: the TEST-1 Tier 1 "Pod builder and admission" item names "one refusal per branch of the CODE-4 `createPod` rules", and the new no-pod-on-failed-pool-read branch is in the CODE-3 `--launch-mode` row, which the CODE-4 `LaunchMode` bullet points at. The design adjudicated this as covered (not-a-site). A later lens may read the refusal rule narrowly; the answer is that the row is a `createPod` rule through that bullet, not a new named input.
FACT: the related-passages lookup timed out (300 s, exit 143) in this round; the citer sweep used grep over every proposal file plus spec/ and docs/.

### [non-spec.8.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: the post-READY service-mode cause against SIGTERM is pinned in TEST-1 **Tier 7a.** by extending the existing pre-READY signal race ("and against a service-mode cause after READY"), asserting the CODE-3 **Pre-READY sequence** exit claim's outcome for the winning claim, with "a cause that finds `signalled`" named as the discriminating order — BECAUSE the race is one CAS on the phase word (`ready` to `signalled` against `ready` to `failed`), which is concurrency and owned by 7a, and the signal-first order is what fails an implementation that exits 1 on every cause or checks-then-exits non-atomically — ALTERNATIVES: a Tier 1 `RunPreReady` named input (duplicates the 7a order and stretches "one subtest per T1 cell" past its home); a post-READY cell in the P5 row (the exit claim bullet already owns post-READY outcomes).
WATCHOUT: do not restore "exactly one exit, after `exitDone`" beside the pre-READY/post-READY signal race. It is false whenever a cause or a gate failure wins `failed` (exit 1, no `exitDone`); the 7a text points at the exit claim's outcome instead. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 **Tier 7a.**; CODE-3 **Pre-READY sequence** "Exit claim." bullet.
WATCHOUT: do not list the service-mode causes (hang, missed ping, `none` row, `ended` from the SIGTERM-path EndProcess) in TEST-1; D-SERVICE and the `--launch-mode` row own them. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 **Adapter inputs** `--launch-mode` row.

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the P5 SIGTERM cell routes `ExitOnSignal` through the `--launch-mode` row and drops its own "the process ends as the kubelet row of §4.7.10 **Signals.** states" clause — BECAUSE the row is the one home of the per-pod `ExitOnSignal` branch (it already carries the kubelet ending for `session`), and the clause asserted the kubelet ending on every pod, against the row's `service` EndProcess branch and the `preconnect-*` empty-registry demotion — ALTERNATIVES: keep the clause scoped to `session` (a second statement of the row's session branch); name the pod kinds on T1 `P5/signal` (the TEST-1 Tier 1 **Adapter inputs** rule already asserts each value's keyed-behavior cell).
DECISION: `createPod` derives `Inputs.LaunchMode` from the Runtime CRD it already holds (`capabilities.preConnect`, `executionMode`) and the pool's `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` read at creation, and a failed pool read on a `preConnect` runtime creates no pod — BECAUSE SPEC-2 **SDK-warm.** fixes the warm-up launch from the breaker state at creation, and `resolveSDKWarm` reads a pool-read error as breaker closed (a launch while the breaker may be open) and a Runtime-read error as not `preConnect`; an unknown breaker state cannot be honored either way, and the Runtime read in `createPod` already fails closed — ALTERNATIVES: pod-warm on a pool-read error (departs from SPEC-2 when the breaker is closed; owner preference is fail closed, and the WarmPoolController retries); keep `resolveSDKWarm` (its best-effort contract is right for the `sdk_connecting` route and wrong for a creation-time fixed flag).
CORRECTS [review-log line 1435 DECISION "the transient-lookup outcome (`preconnect-pod-warm`) is stated once"]: the row no longer renders `preconnect-pod-warm` on a lookup error; a failed pool read on a `preConnect` runtime creates no pod, and the Runtime read is `createPod`'s own, which already fails closed (pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go createPod `get runtime` error).
CORRECTS [review-log line 1427 facet (a) "`createPod` sets `preconnect-sdk-warm` when the Sandbox's `resolveSDKWarm` result is `sdkWarmActive()`"]: the derivation reads the pool's `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` directly; `resolveSDKWarm` stays the WarmPoolController's `sdk_connecting` route read only (summary D-SDKWARM).
FACT: `resolveSDKWarm` sets `disabled` only on a successful pool Get, so a pool read error or NotFound yields breaker closed; a Runtime Get error other than NotFound yields `preConnect=false` — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go resolveSDKWarm (lines 69-98).
WATCHOUT: `createPod`'s other pool reads (`resolveTemplate`, `resourceClassName`) are best-effort and fall back on a miss; the new `sdkWarmDisabled` read is the one pool read that must fail closed, so an implementor folding the reads into one Get must not inherit the fallback — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go resolveTemplate, resourceClassName.
OPEN: the `--launch-mode` row's `service` branch sends `EndProcess` on every SIGTERM, after READY with a session registered included, while the `preconnect-*` branch demotes only an empty registry (CODE-3 SDK-warm bullet) and D-PODEXIT wants a held session's runtime to survive the drain start. Whether a service-mode pod holds a session whose eviction checkpoint needs the runtime is unverified; a later round on the drain lens should decide (pre-existing in the row, not caused by this group's edits).

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: I6 (`uncoveredInputsFields`) fails on every `podspec.Inputs` field that is zero in every render case, so each Inputs field CODE-4 adds has to be set by the supervised maximal case — EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/deployment_boundary_test.go:419-433; deployment_boundary_render_test.go:30-31.
DECISION: CODE-4's deployment-boundary bullet now points ("sets every `Inputs` field this change adds (I6)") instead of listing the fields — BECAUSE the list missed the two ping fields once and would miss the two timeout fields G2 adds; a pointer cannot go stale — ALTERNATIVES: appending the field names to the list (the reviewer's fix; it goes stale on the next field).
DECISION: the P2 wait and the warm-up wait become controller-tunable in the same way as the ping bounds. CODE-4's ping bullet becomes one timing bullet covering four `Inputs` fields, four controller flags, and four `controller.tuning` keys, and the CODE-3 table gains a `--supervise-wait-timeout` row. `--warm-up-timeout` is rendered on every supervised pod, and its `no-spec-value` row is dropped — BECAUSE CODE-4's own stated reason (no spec value, so an operator override is needed) reads on both waits, and standing context **Pre-READY wait covers image pull** shows a slow registry needs the override — ALTERNATIVES: leaving both unrendered with `no-spec-value` rows (contradicts the CODE-4 reason the ping decision relied on); one `--pre-ready-timeout` spanning P2 to P4 (renames a flag, rewrites P2/P3 cells and FIXTURE-1, and lets a cold pull consume the warm-up budget; a redesign rather than a fix).
DECISION: the CODE-3 deferred-row enumeration (line ~126) becomes a pointer to the **Adapter inputs** table rows CODE-3 adds — BECAUSE the list is a second statement of the table and grows with every row.
DECISION: CODE-4 still does not render the supervisor's `--dial-timeout`. CODE-2's default is tied to the `--supervise-wait-timeout` default — BECAUSE standing context **Dial wording** records that CODE-4 does not render it.
UNVERIFIED: whether the dial window has to cover the adapter container's image pull. That depends on the order `buildSidecar` lists the adapter and runtime containers (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go `pod.Spec.Containers`). If the runtime is listed first, an operator raising `--supervise-wait-timeout` gets no matching dial-window override. A later round on CODE-2/CODE-4 should check this.
WATCHOUT: the chart keys are `controller.tuning.superviseWaitTimeout` and `controller.tuning.warmUpTimeout`, beside the two ping keys. TEST-1 Tier 0 and Tier 1 now cite "each duration flag" or "each `controller.tuning` key" CODE-4's timing bullet adds rather than naming them, so do not restore a list.
USEFUL [redesign-remedy-verify.2026-10-23.6]: its closing WATCHOUT on `--warm-up-timeout` identified finding 2 exactly.

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: close the post-READY service-mode cause-versus-SIGTERM coverage gap in TEST-1 **Tier 7a.** only, by replacing the pre-READY race clause and its parenthetical in place: "a signal against the return of each pre-READY gate and against a service-mode cause after READY (randomized interleavings, asserting the CODE-3 **Pre-READY sequence** exit claim's outcome for the claim that wins; a cause that finds `signalled` is the discriminating order)" — BECAUSE the race is concurrency (tier 7a per test-coverage.md), randomized interleavings that force both orders catch both the exit-1-on-every-cause defect and the non-atomic check-then-exit defect, and the replaced parenthetical stops restating the claim ("exactly one exit, after `exitDone`"), which is false whenever `main` or the cause wins `failed` — ALTERNATIVES: a Tier 1 `RunPreReady` named input (rejected: it duplicates the 7a signal-first order, and the post-READY cause is not a T1 cell, so it would stretch "one subtest per T1 cell"); a new T1 cell in the P5 row (rejected: it grows the table that is the one home of pre-READY exits with a post-READY case).
WATCHOUT: do not put the new race in front of the old parenthetical "exactly one exit, after `exitDone`". That wording was already wrong for a gate failure that wins `failed` (review-log WATCHOUT at :953). It would also be wrong for a cause that wins `ready`->`failed`, which exits 1 and never closes `exitDone`. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:144, :266
FACT: in the exit claim bullet (non-spec-changes.md:144), a service-mode cause after READY claims `ready`->`failed` and the handler claims `ready`->`signalled`. So the 7a race is one CAS on the same word as the pre-READY race, and the same test harness, with the signal channel and exit function injected, covers it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:142-144

### [non-spec.8.review-client-surface.1]

FACT: `resolveSDKWarm` returns early with `disabled=false` whenever it reads `preConnect` as false, and that includes a transient Runtime lookup error. `sdkWarmActive()` is then false, so the CODE-3 `--launch-mode` row's transient-error claim (`preconnect-pod-warm`) holds even when createPod's own Runtime Get succeeds. FACT: Reconcile re-resolves the breaker on every pass, while `--launch-mode` is fixed when the pod is created. Breaker drift in either direction converges. A pod-warm pod under DecideSDKWarm reaches idle once its probe passes. An sdk-warm pod under Decide waited for its warm-up connection before READY. Neither case strands a pod. DECISION: I returned no findings under the Kubernetes-idiom lens — BECAUSE the writes match the ownership table (PSC writes SandboxTemplate.spec, WPC sets the immutable pod label once at create, gateway reads it under its existing pod get and writes only SandboxClaim deletes and the drain annotation), no status field carries a command, no finalizer is added, and no session-start path waits on a reconcile — ALTERNATIVES: I considered the eventual-consistency window after a keep-to-restart pool edit, where the PSC mirror lags and new pods are labelled keep. I rejected it because the gate refuses those pods when they are reused, which fails closed and costs only churn.

### [prune-remedy.2026-10-23.7]
DECISION: applied the round-8 introspection prune to non-spec-changes.md (86,316 to 83,856 bytes) and summary.md. These became IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE items, each stating its requirement, the invariant that must hold, and the test that proves it, with no file placement, registration mechanics, or wiring steps: (1) TEST-1 **Tier 0.**, which keeps the CODE-2 binary check with its unverified verdict on a host with no C compiler, every tier-0 gate passing over the added rows and render cases, the controller argv carrying the supervisor image and each timing setting, and the dev profile rendering `--supervisor-image`; and the C harness invocation, constrained to keep `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...` green, which drops the `supervisor/harness_test.go` placement that broke both; (2) the CODE-3 register bullet, now a requirement that every tier-0 gate passes at the end of each checklist step and that the claim register records the wired surfaces, and the CODE-4 deployment-boundary bullets, which keep what the gate asserts on a supervised pod; the register and gate files left the CODE-3 and CODE-4 targets and their summary lines; (3) the CODE-4 timing bullet, now one requirement naming the ping interval, the ping timeout, the supervisor wait timeout, and the warm-up timeout as operator-tunable controller settings rendered to the **Adapter inputs** flags and refused when non-positive, with the reason stated once in summary D-PING and the `Inputs.*` sources in the table replaced by the timing setting; (4) the **Pre-READY sequence** exit claim, replaced by invariants (exactly one path exits; a signal's exit follows the completion of the end; a cause before the listener bind fails pre-READY, one after `srv.Serve` starts takes its **Liveness.** cell, and one between takes exactly one of those; every wait ends), with the cell outcomes kept and the cells reworded to drop the phase words and `exitDone`. The breaker read now has one statement: the `--launch-mode` row reads `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` once at creation and fails closed, and D-SDKWARM points at the row instead of citing `resolveSDKWarm` and `sdkWarmActive`. The §17.8 timing-knob gap is frozen-spec debt (3) — BECAUSE the round-8 introspection found that the design's behavior holds and that each round's fixes to this plumbing introduced the next round's findings, which only the tier runs can settle, and the round-6 prune of the Tier-1 enumeration stopped the same class from recurring — ALTERNATIVES: redesign (no behavioral mechanism is under repair) and halt (premature while one prune remains untried).
DECISION: later rounds do not re-stage plumbing inside these IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE items. A finding against one must show a violated invariant or a missing assertion that the choice states, and a missing file, registration, or wiring step is not a finding — BECAUSE re-staging the plumbing reopens the loop this prune closes — ALTERNATIVES: none.
WATCHOUT: the WarmPoolController's per-pass `resolveSDKWarm` read chooses the `sdk_connecting` route and is not the launch-mode read; [non-spec.8] found that breaker drift between the two converges. It is unchanged and unstaged.
### [prune-remedy.2026-10-23.8]
DECISION: post-prune review of `[prune-remedy.2026-10-23.7]` restored the assertions and constraints the prune dropped: (1) the CODE-4 timing choice now binds the supervisor wait and warm-up chart keys to `controller.tuning.superviseWaitTimeout` and `controller.tuning.warmUpTimeout`, which the DOC-1 runbook step names, and states that a stock render leaves the defaults, which TEST-1 **Tier 0.** asserts by the argv omitting an unset setting; (2) the deployment-boundary bullet keeps the environment rule on an unsupervised pod's runtime `Env`; (3) the **Pre-READY sequence** exit bullet names a listen or serve error rather than the bind as a racing end, states that both outcomes for a cause between the bind and `srv.Serve` retire the pod, and replaces the wait invariant with one a reviewer can check against the synchronization (a path waits only for an event that the decided exit owner produces), and T1 `P5/failure` gains a cause set between the bind and `srv.Serve`, the window that the retired claim's "between the bind and the claim" case covered; (4) the tier-1 pod-builder cases name a failed pool read for a `preConnect` runtime, which pins the fail-closed launch-mode read — BECAUSE each was a requirement the prune removed without moving to an invariant or a kept assertion, or a constraint that staged text elsewhere in the proposal already fixes — ALTERNATIVES: leave the chart keys open and edit DOC-1 to name no key (rejected: the runbook step exists to name the knob an operator raises).
